# A quadratic bound for Jacobsthal's function

Coordinate `EN-101-000-0000-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M1-B-AL-H-0058` | `PM-021.1` | status ACTIVE | original slug `A-quadratic-bound-for-Jacobsthals-function-September-25-2026` | date 2026-09-25 | derived from the openai/math preprint A-quadratic-bound-for-Jacobsthals-function-September-25-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). Publication status: DRAFT.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.node.v2 -->
```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "Let $`h(k)`$ be the least integer such that every interval of $`h(k)`$ consecutive integers contains an integer coprime to any prescribed positive integer having at most <i>k</i> distinct prime divisors. We prove $`h(k)\\ll k^2/(\\log\\log(3k))^2`$, giving an affirmative answer to Jacobsthal's quadratic-bound question.",
```

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Let $`h(k)`$ be the least integer such that every interval of $`h(k)`$ consecutive integers contains an integer coprime to any prescribed positive integer having at most <i>k</i> distinct prime divisors. We prove $`h(k)\ll k^2/(\log\log(3k))^2`$, giving an affirmative answer to Jacobsthal's quadratic-bound question.

- **claim-uniform-jacobsthal-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There is an absolute constant $C>0$ such that $$h(k)\le C\frac{k^2}{(\log\log(3k))^2} \qquad\text{for every integer }k\ge1.$$
  - Explanation: Bounds the largest coprime interval gap uniformly over the prime divisors and the interval translation, with an iterated-logarithmic saving.
- **claim-arbitrary-class-survivors** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-survivors)): There are absolute constants $c>0$ and $z_0$ with the following property. For every real $z\ge z_0$ and every choice of a residue class $a_p\pmod p$ for each prime $p\le z$, $$\label{eq:main-survivors} \#\{1\le n\le Y:n\not\equiv a_p\pmod p\text{ for every }p\le z\} \ge c\frac{YV_0}{B^2}.$$
  - Explanation: Gives a quantitative survivor lower bound for arbitrary prescribed classes at the prime cutoff.
- **claim-fundamental-sieve-estimate** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-fundamental)): Let $\mathcal P$ be any subset of the primes at most $v$. Suppose that intersection counts for bad conditions indexed by squarefree products $l$ of primes in $\mathcal P$ have the form $$Xg(l)+r_l,$$ where $g$ is multiplicative and $$0\le g(p)\le\frac2p,\qquad g(p)<1,\qquad g(2)\le\frac12 \quad\text{if }2\in\mathcal P.$$ There are absolute constants $c,C>0$ and $s_0$ such that for $s\ge s_0$ the count avoiding all the bad conditions is $$\label{eq:fundamental-input} X\prod_{p\in\mathcal P}(1-g(p))\bigl(1+O(e^{-cs})\bigr) +O\!\left( \sum_{\substack{l\le v^s\\l\text{ squarefree}\\p\mid l\Rightarrow p\in\mathcal P}} \tau(l)^C\|r_l\|\right).$$ The same statement holds for nonnegative weighted counts. A local condition that makes the sifted set empty may instead be removed from consideration as an exactly empty case.
  - Explanation: Approximates a sifted count by its local-density product, with a sieve error and a weighted intersection remainder.
- **claim-prime-distribution-inputs** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-prime-inputs)): The following estimates will be used, with absolute constants unless fixed subscripts indicate a dependence.* 1. *There is $c>0$ such that $$\pi(v)=\mathop{\mathrm{li}}(v)+O\!\left(\frac v{\log v}e^{-c\sqrt{\log v}}\right), \qquad \prod_{p\le v}(1-1/p) =\frac{e^{-\gamma}}{\log v} \bigl(1+O(e^{-c\sqrt{\log v}})\bigr).$$ Decreasing $c$ absorbs harmless powers of $\log v$.* 2. *For every fixed $A,M>0$, uniformly for $q\le(\log v)^A$ and $(a,q)=1$, $$\pi(v;q,a)=\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{li}}(v)}{\varphi(q)} +O_{A,M}\!\left(\frac v{(\log v)^M}\right).$$ The implied constants may be ineffective.* 3. *For $(a,q)=1$ and $H/q$ exceeding a suitable absolute constant, $$\#\{x<p\le x+H:p\equiv a\pmod q\} \ll\frac{H}{\varphi(q)\log(H/q)}.$$* 4. *Uniformly for $v^{9/10}\le H\le v$ as $v\to\infty$, $$\#\{v-H<p\le v\}\ge(1-o(1))\frac H{\log v}.$$
  - Explanation: Collects the prime-counting, product, progression and short-interval estimates used throughout the argument, retaining possible ineffectivity in progression asymptotics.
- **claim-small-prime-progression-counts** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-small-sieve)): Let one forbidden class be given at every prime $p\le u$, where $u\to\infty$. Count the points in a progression segment whose coordinate interval has real length $J$, and whose step is coprime to every prime $p\le u$. Put $V_{\mathrm{sm}}(u)=\prod_{p\le u}(1-1/p)$. For each fixed $\eta>0$, uniformly when $J\ge u^\eta$, its sifted count $N$ satisfies $$\label{eq:small-upper} N\ll_\eta JV_{\mathrm{sm}}(u).$$ There are absolute $c_1,C_1>0$ and a sufficiently large fixed $v_0$ such that, writing $v=\log_uJ$, uniformly for $v\ge v_0$, $$\label{eq:small-relative} \left\|\frac{N}{JV_{\mathrm{sm}}(u)}-1\right\|\le C_1e^{-c_1v}.$$ All estimates are independent of the progression’s initial point, step and forbidden classes.
  - Explanation: Provides an absolute upper sieve for admitted progression lengths and a relative approximation when the logarithmic length is sufficiently large.
- **claim-parity-tree-lower-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-tree-lower-bound)): The alternating sum of the $N_d$ over all included nodes is a lower bound for $S_1(B)$. More generally, one may stop at any prefix-free collection of included lower nodes, use their exact counts $S_d(b)$, and expand only the other nodes. The resulting expression is still a lower bound for $S_1(B)$. Every included node satisfies $J_d\ge w^{a_\star}$.
  - Explanation: Justifies the alternating lower bound and replacement of prefix-free lower subtrees by exact counts, while preserving a positive progression-length margin.
- **claim-positive-future-integrals** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-future-integrals)): The functions $a,c$ are positive and satisfy, with absolute comparison constants, $$\begin{aligned} a(s)+c(s)&=c_0(s),& a(s)&\asymp c_0(s),& c(s)&\asymp c_0(s)\qquad(s\ge1),\label{eq:functions-deficits}\\ s a(s)&=\int_{s-1}^{\infty}c(t)\,dt &&(s\ge2),\label{eq:functions-future-a}\\ s c(s)&=\int_{s-1}^{\infty}a(t)\,dt &&(s\ge1).\label{eq:functions-future-c} \end{aligned}$$ For $u\ge2$, $$\label{eq:functions-dickman-ratio} \frac1{4u^2}\le\frac{\rho(u)}{\rho(u-1)}\le\frac1u .$$ Moreover, for each fixed $\lambda>0$, there is $U_\lambda$ such that $$\label{eq:functions-dickman-logderivative} -\frac{\rho'(u)}{\rho(u)}\ge\lambda\qquad(u\ge U_\lambda).$$
  - Explanation: Establishes positive sieve deficits, their future-integral identities and Dickman comparisons needed to control the delay functions.
- **claim-derivative-weight-normalisation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-derivative-weights)): The derivative weights are positive and nonincreasing on their indicated ranges. If $i'$ denotes the type opposite to $i$, then $$\label{eq:functions-derivative-integrals} \phi_i(s)=\int_{m_i(s)}^{\infty}W_0(t)\phi_{i'}(t)\,dt.$$ This holds also on the specified starting extensions. On a tail, $$\label{eq:functions-phi-dickman} \phi_\mathrm e(s)\asymp\rho(s-2),\qquad \phi_\mathrm o(s)\asymp\rho(s-2).$$ There are absolute constants $C,q$ such that the absolute logarithmic derivatives, where they exist, and the ratios $\phi_{i'}(m_i(s))/\phi_i(s)$ are at most $C(1+s)^q$. In particular, for $u,v\le S$ in the indicated domain of the same weight, with $S\ge3$, $$\|\log\phi_i(u)-\log\phi_i(v)\| \le C(1+S)^q\|u-v\|.$$ For each fixed $\lambda>0$, the negative logarithmic derivatives of both weights are at least $\lambda$ on a sufficiently remote tail.
  - Explanation: Proves positivity, integral normalisation and derivative control for the weights that convert harmonic paths into probability paths.
- **claim-regeneration-cycle-moments** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-regeneration)): The time to first regeneration has an exponential moment uniformly over <a href="#eq:functions-start-range" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:functions-start-range">[eq:functions-start-range]</a>. The cycles strictly after one regeneration through the next inclusive are iid. Their lengths $\tau$ have a finite exponential moment. Their total log decrements $$G=\sum_{j=1}^{\tau}g(s_j)$$ are positive, nonarithmetic, and have a finite exponential moment for some positive parameter. In particular $0<\mathbb EG<\infty$. Each complete cycle satisfies $G\ge g(3)=\log(4/3)$.
  - Explanation: Supplies independent regeneration cycles, exponential moments and nonarithmetic decrements for the renewal argument.
- **claim-invariant-cycle-occupation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-invariant)): On the ordinary even and odd state spaces, respectively, the finite raw invariant densities are $$\label{eq:functions-invariant-densities} \pi_\mathrm e(s)=\phi_\mathrm e(s)\quad(s\ge2),\qquad \pi_\mathrm o(s)=(1-s^{-2})\phi_\mathrm o(s)\quad(s\ge1).$$ Put $$\beta=\int_1^3\pi_\mathrm o(s)\,ds=\frac{4A}{3}.$$ For every nonnegative measurable state function $Q$, $$\label{eq:functions-cycle-occupation} \mathbb E\sum_{j=1}^{\tau}Q(i_j,s_j) =\frac1\beta\sum_i\int Q(i,s)\pi_i(s)\,ds.$$ In particular, if $$M_g=\sum_i\int\pi_i(s)g(s)\,ds,$$ then $\mathbb EG=M_g/\beta$, and $$\label{eq:mg-bound} 0<M_g\le4e^\gamma.$$
  - Explanation: Identifies the raw invariant densities and relates their integrals to expected occupation during a regeneration cycle.
- **claim-continuous-compact-occupation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-occupation)): For an admissible compact test and starts satisfying <a href="#eq:functions-start-range" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:functions-start-range">[eq:functions-start-range]</a>, $$\label{eq:functions-occupation-limit} \lim_{v\to\infty} \mathbb E_{s_{\rm in}}\sum_{n\ge0}F_{i_n}(v-T_n,s_n) = \frac1{M_g}\sum_i\int_{\mathbb R}\int F_i(u,s)\pi_i(s)\,ds\,du.$$ The convergence is uniform in $s_{\rm in}\in[1.99,2.3]$. The state integrals on the right use the ordinary invariant ranges in <a href="#eq:functions-invariant-densities" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:functions-invariant-densities">[eq:functions-invariant-densities]</a>.
  - Explanation: Turns regeneration and the key renewal theorem into a uniform compact occupation limit.
- **claim-uniform-occupation-band-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-band-bounds)): For every fixed $h>0$ there is $C_h<\infty$ with the following properties. For any nonnegative measurable state function $Q$ and any $v\in\mathbb R$, a regenerating start satisfies $$\label{eq:functions-band-from-regeneration} \mathbb E\sum_{n\ge1}Q(i_n,s_n) \mathbf 1_{\{T_n\in[v,v+h]\}} \le C_h\sum_i\int Q(i,s)\pi_i(s)\,ds.$$ From an even start in <a href="#eq:functions-start-range" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:functions-start-range">[eq:functions-start-range]</a>, the same bound holds for the sum over $n\ge2$, uniformly in the starting ratio. The density of the first odd state is at most $$C\,\mathbf 1_{\{s\ge0.98\}}\phi_\mathrm o(s).$$ In particular, uniformly in $r_{\rm in}>0$, $R>0$, and $\ell\in\{1,2\}$, $$\label{eq:functions-inverse-phi-band} \mathbb E\sum_{n\ge1} \frac{\mathbf 1_{\{R\le r_n\le eR,\ s_n<r_n/\ell\}}} {\phi_{i_n}(s_n)} \ll R+1.$$ Including the starting state in the last sum changes its bound by at most an absolute constant.
  - Explanation: Bounds occupation in logarithmic gap bands, including rewards divided by derivative weights.
- **claim-prescribed-marked-even-visits** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-marked-visits)): Fix $b_0>0$ and $\varepsilon>0$. There is a constant $C_\varepsilon>10$, independent of $b_0$, such that, whenever $b_1\ge C_\varepsilon b_0$ is fixed, the following event has probability at least $1-\varepsilon-o(1)$ as $r_{\rm in}\to\infty$, uniformly in <a href="#eq:functions-start-range" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:functions-start-range">[eq:functions-start-range]</a>. At a regenerating odd state with pre-draw gap in $[10b_0,b_1]$, the next draw is an even arrival satisfying $$s\in[2.08,2.14],\qquad x\in[2b_0,b_1/2].$$ The proof yields the smaller ratio interval $[2.09,2.13]$; thus the stated ratio window has positive slack. The arriving gap exceeds $6b_0$, so, if $6b_0>K$, such a visit precedes every subsequent arrival of gap at most $K$.
  - Explanation: Makes a suitable even arrival overwhelmingly likely when the prescribed gap window is sufficiently wide.
- **claim-prime-harmonic-quadrature** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-prime-harmonic)): There are absolute positive constants $C,c$ such that, on any exponent interval with lower endpoint $u\geq1/2$, $$\label{eq:prime-harmonic} \sum_{x(p)\ \mathrm{in\ the\ interval}}\frac1p =\int_{\mathrm{interval}}\frac{dx}{x}+O(E(u)), \qquad E(u)=C\exp(-c\sqrt{u\log w}).$$ The error is uniform in the upper endpoint and in either endpoint convention. In particular, the total weight of all decreasing prime prefixes with exponents in $(\ell,B]$, with no admission restrictions, is $O(B)$.
  - Explanation: Approximates reciprocal-prime sums by harmonic integrals with endpoint-uniform errors.
- **claim-negligible-high-state-histories** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-high-states)): Fix $K>0$ and $A>0$. The total original harmonic weight of prefixes ending at gap at most $K$ and having some earlier or terminal ratio greater than $S$ is $O_{K,A}(B^{-A})$. More generally, for any fixed $c'>0$, the sum of $h(\mathfrak p)\exp(-c'r(\mathfrak p))$ over prefixes having such a high state is $O_{A,c'}(B^{-A})$. Both conclusions also hold in the continuous harmonic model, uniformly in the starting ratios under consideration.
  - Explanation: Removes histories with excessively large ratios from compact and exponentially weighted path sums.
- **claim-discrete-continuous-prime-coupling** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-prime-coupling)): Choose sufficiently small absolute positive constants in the following order. For a permitted prime child of a state of type $i$, ratio $s$, and gap $r$, put $t=r/x(p)-1$ and give that child mass $$\label{eq:prime-tilt} q(p\mid r,s,i)=\frac1p\left(\frac{t+1}{t}\right)^2 \frac{\phi_{i'}(t)}{\phi_i(s)}.$$ Only children with $x(p)>\ell$ and $t\leq S$ are retained. For $\epsilon_w=\exp(-c_2\sqrt{\log w})$ their total mass is at most $1+\epsilon_w$. Dividing by $1+\epsilon_w$ and assigning the remaining mass to a cemetery state defines a killed Markov chain.* *This chain can be coupled with the continuous chain <a href="#eq:tilted-kernel" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:tilted-kernel">[eq:tilted-kernel]</a>, killed at the first draw with $x\leq\ell$ or $t>S$, so that the probability of failure is at most $\exp(-c_3\sqrt{\log w})$. On success the chains have the same number of arrived-at states and matching exits. At corresponding states, $$\label{eq:prime-coupling-errors} \|s-\widetilde s\|\leq h,\qquad \left\|\log\frac r{\widetilde r}\right\|\leq C N h,\qquad h=\frac1{\lceil\exp(c_4\sqrt{\log w})\rceil}.$$ All statements are uniform in $s_{\mathrm{st}}\in[1.99,2.3]$ and in the endpoint conventions stated above. For a length-$m$ retained prefix, if $\mathbb P_w(\mathfrak p)$ denotes its probability in the killed chain, then the exact weight identity is $$\label{eq:prime-weight} h(\mathfrak p)=\mathbb P_w(\mathfrak p)(1+\epsilon_w)^m \left(\frac{r(\mathfrak p)}{r_{\mathrm{st}}}\right)^2 \frac{\phi_\mathrm e(s_{\mathrm{st}})}{\phi_i(s(\mathfrak p))}, \qquad (1+\epsilon_w)^m=1+o(1).$$
  - Explanation: Defines a killed prime chain, couples it to the continuous tilted chain and retains an exact telescoping conversion to harmonic weights.
- **claim-prime-chain-marginal-majorants** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-discrete-marginals)): For the killed chain of Proposition <a href="#prop:prime-coupling" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:prime-coupling">17</a>, the probability of a nonexit bin with left endpoint $t$ at step $2\leq n\leq N$ is bounded, with an absolute constant, by $$\label{eq:prime-marginals} \begin{cases} C h\phi_\mathrm e(t),&n\ \hbox{even},\\[2mm] C h\phi_\mathrm o(t)\bigl(1-t^{-2}+3hW_0(t)\bigr),&n\ \hbox{odd}. \end{cases}$$ At step $1$ the bound is $C hW_0(t)\phi_\mathrm o(t)$, on the slightly extended range $t\geq0.98$. The constants are uniform in the starting ratios and in $w$ sufficiently large.
  - Explanation: Adds pointwise marginal bounds needed when inverse derivative weights prevent using total variation alone.
- **claim-prime-path-occupation** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-prime-occupation)): The following assertions hold uniformly for $s_{\mathrm{st}}\in[1.99,2.3]$ and $\ell\in\{1,2\}$.* 1. *For fixed $K$, the original harmonic mass of prefixes with terminal gap at most $K$ is $O_K(B^{-2})$.* 2. *Consider any selection of such compact-ending prefixes whose low-state histories imply an event $\mathcal A$ in the killed prime chain. Their total weight is at most $$\label{eq:prime-compact-event} C_K B^{-2}\mathbb P_w(\mathcal A)+O_{K,A}(B^{-A})$$ for every fixed $A>0$. One may take $\mathcal A$ to be the event that at least one selected compact prefix occurs.* 3. *For every fixed $c'>0$, $$\label{eq:prime-tail} \lim_{K\to\infty}\limsup_{w\to\infty} B^2\sum_{\substack{\mathfrak p\ \mathrm{standard}\\r(\mathfrak p)>K}} h(\mathfrak p)e^{-c'r(\mathfrak p)}=0.$$ The same compact and tail assertions hold for continuous original harmonic weights.* 4. *Let $H_i(r,s)$ be bounded continuous functions supported in a fixed compact positive gap interval and a fixed compact ratio interval. Fixed interval boundaries and the indicator $s<r/\ell$ are also permitted, with one-sided continuous extension before imposing that indicator, as in Proposition <a href="#prop:occupation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:occupation">11</a>. Then $$\begin{aligned} \label{eq:prime-occupation} &\frac{r_{\mathrm{st}}^2}{\phi_\mathrm e(s_{\mathrm{st}})} \sum_{\mathfrak p}h(\mathfrak p) H_{i(\mathfrak p)}(r(\mathfrak p),s(\mathfrak p))\notag\\ &\hspace{8mm}\longrightarrow \frac1{M_g}\sum_{i\in\{\mathrm e,\mathrm o\}} \int_0^\infty\int \frac{r^2\pi_i(s)}{\phi_i(s)}H_i(r,s) \mathbf 1_{s<r/\ell}\,ds\,\frac{dr}{r}, \end{aligned}$$ where the inner domains are $s\geq2$ for $i=\mathrm e$ and $s\geq1$ for $i=\mathrm o$. The convergence is uniform in the starting ratios.
  - Explanation: Transfers compact occupation, event-weighted compact bounds and exponential tail control to actual prime paths.
- **claim-summed-reference-quadrature-error** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-ref-residual)): Along the standard prime paths with $x>2$ from the root, $$\label{eq:ref-residual-sum} \sum_{\mathfrak p}h(\mathfrak p) \left\|R_{i(\mathfrak p)}^{(w)} (r(\mathfrak p),b(\mathfrak p))\right\|=o(B^{-2}).$$ The same assertion is uniform for lower starting ratios in $[1.99,2.3]$ and starting cutoffs $B$ tending to infinity with the parameters of <a href="#eq:parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:parameters">[eq:parameters]</a>.
  - Explanation: Shows that the accumulated discrete quadrature residual is negligible at the scale of the reference margin.
- **claim-boundary-anomaly-convergence-decay** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-ref-anomaly)): On every fixed compact subset of their respective domains, $\delta_i^{(w)}$ converges uniformly to the continuous function $\delta_i$. Uniformly for sufficiently large $w$, $$\label{eq:ref-anomaly-decay} \|\delta_i^{(w)}(r)\|+\|\delta_i(r)\| \ll\exp(-c r\log(r+2)).$$
  - Explanation: Combines compact convergence of the boundary polynomial with rapid decay of its discrepancy from the sieve benchmark.
- **claim-positive-reference-root-margin** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-reference-margin)): At the root of the reference tree, $$\label{eq:ref-margin} \frac{B^2P_\mathrm e(r_0,B)}{e^\gamma} \longrightarrow -a_\star+\frac{2I}{M_g}>0.02.$$ In particular, there is an absolute $c_L>0$ such that $B^2P_\mathrm e(r_0,B)\geq c_L$ for all sufficiently large $z$.
  - Explanation: Evaluates the signed boundary correction and proves an eventual positive reference contribution at the root.
- **claim-local-reference-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-local-reference)): For the continuous reference polynomial, $$\label{eq:ref-local-cont} Q_\mathrm e(r,b)=\frac{f(r/b)}b+O(b^{-2}) \qquad\left(b\longrightarrow\infty,\quad2\leq r/b\leq2.22\right),$$ uniformly in the displayed ratios. On every fixed cutoff window $[b_0,b_1]$ with $b_0$ sufficiently large, the corresponding discrete estimate is $$\label{eq:ref-local-prime} \frac{P_\mathrm e(r,b)}{V(b)} =f(r/b)+O(1/b)+o_{w\to\infty}(1), \qquad b\in[b_0,b_1],\quad2\leq r/b\leq2.22.$$ The constant in $O(1/b)$ is absolute, the last convergence is uniform on the fixed window, and either prime endpoint convention is allowed.
  - Explanation: Controls continuous and discrete reference values near the lower-sieve threshold, with the cutoff window fixed before the prime limit.
- **claim-recent-discrepant-edge** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-edge-witness)): Fix $K$ and $0<\varepsilon<1$. One can choose a fixed $K_*>K+10$ such that, with $$H_e=\lceil 2K_*+10\rceil,\qquad \delta=\frac{\varepsilon}{4H_e},$$ the following holds for all sufficiently large $z$, uniformly in the prescribed residue classes. If an included endpoint $d$ has $r_d\le K$ and $\|N_d/\mu_d-1\|>\varepsilon$, then one of its last $H_e$ edges, with parent $d'$ and child prime $u$, satisfies $$\label{eq:boxes-witness} \left\|N_{d'u}-\frac{N_{d'}}u\right\| >\delta\frac{\mu_{d'}}u, \qquad x(u),\ \log_w J_{d'}\le 2K_*+3, \qquad \log_w J_{d'u}\ge a_\star.$$
  - Explanation: Locates a recent discrepant edge whenever a compact endpoint has a large relative counting error.
- **claim-regular-independent-prime-boxes** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-regular-boxes)): Fix $K$ and $K_*>K+10$. For every $\vartheta>0$ there are fixed constants $C_{\rm len}$ and $\alpha_{\min}>0$, followed by constants $\xi_0>0$ and $C_{\rm th}<\infty$, with the following properties. They are chosen before $z$ tends to infinity, and $C_{\rm th}$ is independent of $0<\xi\le\xi_0$. Put $$\label{eq:boxes-delta} \Delta=2C_{\rm len}\xi.$$ For each fixed $0<\xi\le\xi_0$ and sufficiently large $z$, there is a family $\mathcal G$ of regular included endpoints with $r_d\le K$ such that $$\label{eq:boxes-exception} \mathfrak m\bigl(\{d\text{ included}:r_d\le K\}\setminus\mathcal G\bigr) \le \frac{\vartheta+C_{\rm th}\Delta+o_\xi(1)}{B^2}.$$ The term $o_\xi(1)$ tends to zero after all fixed parameters, including $\xi$, have been chosen. The statement is uniform in the prescribed residue classes. The regular endpoints and their covering full boxes have these additional properties.* 1. *Every regular endpoint has length at most $C_{\rm len}\log B$ and has an isolated position. Every used search bin, including its isolated bin, has multiplicity one. Every position satisfying $$x_j\le2K_*+3,\qquad r_{j-1}\le2K_*+5$$ also has multiplicity one. The isolated position precedes every edge supplied by Lemma <a href="#lem:edge-witness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:edge-witness">25</a>.* 2. *For every nonempty even prefix of a regular endpoint, $$\label{eq:boxes-threshold-clearance} r_j-\mathcal H(x_j)>10\Delta.$$ Moving prime choices anywhere within their fixed bins changes any prefix gap by at most $\Delta$, and changes a threshold expression $r_j-\mathcal H(x_j)$ by at most $\Delta+2h$. In particular the representative gaps and exponents of every regular box satisfy $$\label{eq:boxes-representative-safety} r_j^{\rm rep}\ge \mathcal H(x_j^{\rm rep})+3\Delta \quad\text{at every nonempty even prefix}.$$* 3. *Let $\mathfrak B$ be the distinct full boxes containing at least one regular endpoint. There is a constant $C_K^{\rm box}$, depending only on a fixed enlargement of the endpoint compact range and the allowed starting ratios, such that $$\label{eq:boxes-expanded-mass} \sum_{\mathcal B\in\mathfrak B}\mathfrak m(\mathcal B) \le\frac{C_K^{\rm box}}{B^2}.$$ In particular this constant is independent of $\xi$, $\vartheta$, and the total number of boxes. Every tuple in these boxes is a standard path from initial gap $r_0+1$, ends at gap at most $K+2$ for that starting gap, and has its actual final $\log_wJ\ge3a_\star/4$.* 4. *In a regular box, declare an edge a candidate if it is among the last $H_e=\lceil2K_*+10\rceil$ positions, is after the isolated position, has multiplicity one, and can satisfy the size bounds in <a href="#eq:boxes-witness" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:boxes-witness">[eq:boxes-witness]</a> for some tuple in the box. There are at most $H_e$ candidates, and every witness for a regular endpoint is among them. Throughout the whole box of each candidate, writing its parent as $d'=pq$ and its prime as $u$, one has $$\label{eq:boxes-candidate-slack} \log_w\frac{Y}{pq}\le2K_*+4, \qquad \log_w\frac{Y}{pqu}\ge\frac{3a_\star}{4}.$$ The bins for $p$ and $u$ have lower endpoints $R$ and $U$ satisfying $$\label{eq:boxes-prime-ranges} z^{\alpha_{\min}}\le R\le z^{\alpha_{\max}}, \qquad w\le U\le w^{2K_*+4}.$$ The choices of $p$, $q$, $u$, and the descendants after this edge are independent under the full-box measure. All their prime factors are disjoint. If $S_q$ is the product of the bin upper endpoints for the factors of $q$, with their specified multiplicities, then $$\label{eq:boxes-sq} 1\le\frac{S_q}{q}\le(1+\xi)^{C_{\rm len}\log B}.$$* 5. *Every regular box has at least $c_7\log w$ search positions, for an absolute $c_7>0$ and sufficiently large $z$. These positions have distinct bins. All its nonsearch positions follow all its search positions.
  - Explanation: Organises regular endpoints into full boxes with independent prime choices, admission clearance and a summed mass bound independent of the width.
- **claim-witnessing-edge-inverse-list** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-inverse)): Fix the parameters in <a href="#eq:inverse-box-ranges" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:inverse-box-ranges">[eq:inverse-box-ranges]</a>– <a href="#eq:inverse-q-range" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:inverse-q-range">[eq:inverse-q-range]</a>, the witnessing threshold $\delta>0$, and $0<\sigma<1$. There are constants $c_p>0$, a list-size bound $F<\infty$, and $\xi_{\rm inv}>0$, independent of the eventual choice of fixed $0<\xi\le\xi_{\rm inv}$, with the following property for all sufficiently large $z$.* *The full candidate box has a list $\mathcal A$ of at most $F$ reduced rationals $A/D$ satisfying $$D\le R\mathcal Z^{10},\qquad \|A\|\le S_qR\mathcal Z^{10},\qquad \#\{p\in\mathcal P:p\text{ aligns with }A/D\}\ge c_p n_p . \label{eq:inverse-list}$$ Outside a set of normalized harmonic box measure at most $\sigma$, every occurrence of $$\left\|N_{pqu}-\frac{N_{pq}}u\right\| >\delta\,\frac{YV_0}{pqu} \label{eq:inverse-bad-edge}$$ has a rational $A/D\in\mathcal A$ for which $$p\text{ aligns with }A/D, \qquad D q_E(A/D)\le R\mathcal Z^{10}. \label{eq:inverse-structure}$$ The exceptional measure is relative to the whole box, rather than to the subset on which <a href="#eq:inverse-bad-edge" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:inverse-bad-edge">[eq:inverse-bad-edge]</a> holds.
  - Explanation: Forces most discrepant candidate edges to align with a rational from a fixed bounded list for the whole box.
- **claim-nonlinear-lattice-point-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-nonlinear-points)): Let $P\in\mathbb R[X_1,X_2]$ be nonzero and of degree at most $D_0\ge1$, and let $S\ge1$. The number of lattice points in $[0,S]^2$ lying on at least one nonlinear irreducible component of $P=0$, with irreducibility taken over $\mathbb C$, is $$O\!\left(D_0^4(1+S^{2/3})\right).$$ The implied constant is absolute, independently of the coefficients of $P$. No bound is asserted for points lying only on line components.
  - Explanation: Bounds lattice points on nonlinear complex-irreducible components independently of polynomial coefficients, allowing the inverse argument to extract a line.
- **claim-independent-and-stable-sieve-variance** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-random-variance)): Choose, independently at each prime $t\le w$, one uniformly distributed forbidden residue class. Let $S$ count the integers in an interval $\mathcal J$ of cardinality $J$ that avoid all these classes. For every fixed $\delta>0$, uniformly for $J\ge w^\delta$, $$\mathbb ES=JV_0,\qquad \mathbb E(S-JV_0)^2=o((JV_0)^2) \quad(w\longrightarrow\infty).$$ The assertion has the following stable version. Let $\mu$ be a finite positive measure carrying indicators $I_j$, $j\in\mathcal J$, and let $S=\sum_j I_j$. Suppose, with an absolute fixed implied constant, $$\mu(\Omega)=1+O(\zeta),\qquad \int I_j\,d\mu=V_0(1+O(\zeta)),$$ and, for distinct $i,j$, the joint probability has the upper bound $$\int I_iI_j\,d\mu \le(1+O(\zeta))\,\mathbb P_{\rm ind}(I_i=I_j=1).$$ Here $\mathbb P_{\rm ind}$ denotes the independent forbidden-class model in the first part of the lemma. A zero probability on the right requires exact zero on the left. For $0\le\zeta\le1/2$, $$\int(S-JV_0)^2\,d\mu \ll\bigl(\zeta+o(1)\bigr)(JV_0)^2,$$ uniformly in the same range of $J$.
  - Explanation: Proves concentration for independent forbidden classes and a stable second-moment estimate under the stated domination conditions.
- **claim-large-effective-size-variance-alternative** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-structured-variance)): Fix the compact-box parameters, the inverse-estimate parameters and a relative-error threshold $\varepsilon>0$. For every fixed $\sigma_2>0$ there is a fixed sufficiently large $C_s$, independent of sufficiently small fixed $\xi$, with the following property. For every candidate box, every choice of its factors other than the isolated prime, and every rational on its fixed candidate list for which <a href="#eq:variance-structure-size" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:variance-structure-size">[eq:variance-structure-size]</a> holds, suppose $$T>w^{C_s}.$$ For sufficiently large $z$, fewer than $\sigma_2 n_p$ primes $p\in\mathcal P$ both align to $A/D$ and satisfy $$\|N_{pq_f}/\mu_{pq_f}-1\|>\varepsilon.$$ The estimate is uniform in the box, the fixed prime choices, the rational and the forbidden classes. The threshold on $z$ may depend on fixed $\xi$. The fraction is measured against all primes of $\mathcal P$.
  - Explanation: Shows that a large effective modulus or intercept makes bad aligning isolated primes exceptional.
- **claim-hard-endpoint-classification** (classification, [source](#stmt-cor-hard-endpoints)): Fix the compact range and $\varepsilon>0$. For every prescribed positive $\rho$, the inverse exception parameter and then the variance exception parameter can be chosen so that, for a fixed $C_s$ independent of sufficiently small fixed $\xi$, the following holds for all sufficiently large $z$. Consider the actual included regular compact endpoints covered by the full boxes of <a href="#prop:regular-boxes" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:regular-boxes">26</a>. Except for a set of these endpoints of total harmonic mass at most $\rho B^{-2}$, every such bad endpoint $$\|N_d/\mu_d-1\|>\varepsilon$$ has a witnessing candidate and a rational on that candidate’s fixed list for which the parent is structured, the isolated prime aligns, and $$\label{eq:variance-hard} Dq_{f,E}\le w^{C_s}, \qquad \|C_0\|/R\le w^{C_s}.$$ For each such endpoint all its prime factors with exponent $x(t)>C_s$ align to this same rational, including the isolated prime. The rational satisfies the global bounds $$\label{eq:variance-global-height} D\le w^{C_s}, \qquad \|A\|\le Yw^{C_s+2}.$$ The enlarged full boxes are used only to dominate measure: the assertion concerns the actual included endpoints, rather than every tuple in those enlarged boxes. The exceptional mass can be bounded before choosing $\xi$; only the eventual threshold on $z$ is allowed to depend on fixed $\xi$.
  - Explanation: Classifies almost all remaining bad regular endpoints by a rational of small effective size and global height.
- **claim-aligned-progression-bin-average** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-stops-aligned-average)): Fix $C_s$. Take $b_0$ sufficiently large in terms of $C_s$, and let $b_1>b_0$ be fixed. For every sufficiently small fixed $\xi>0$, the following holds for all sufficiently large $z$. Let $A/D$ be eligible in the sense of <a href="#eq:stops-eligible" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:stops-eligible">[eq:stops-eligible]</a>, let $P=w^b$ be a prime with $b\in[b_0-1,b_1]$, and let $q'$ be a squarefree product of primes at most $z$ whose least prime factor is $P$ and all of whose prime factors align to $A/D$. Let $\mathcal P$ be a search bin, none of whose primes divides $q'$. Suppose that, for every $p\in\mathcal P$, $$\label{eq:stops-length-exponents} J_p=\frac{Y}{pq'},\qquad 2.03\le\log_PJ_p\le2.19.$$ Define $T_p$ to count the integers $1\le n\le Y$ satisfying $Dn\equiv A\pmod{pq'}$ and avoiding the prescribed classes at every prime $t<P$. Then $$\label{eq:stops-all-primes-lower} \sum_{p\in\mathcal P}T_p \ge .39V_{\rm all}(P)\sum_{p\in\mathcal P}J_p.$$ There is also an absolute constant $C_U$, independent of $C_s,b_0,b_1$ and of sufficiently small fixed $\xi$, such that $$\label{eq:stops-individual-upper} T_p\le C_UJ_pV_{\rm all}(P)\qquad(p\in\mathcal P).$$ Both estimates are uniform in the eligible rational, the bin, the product $q'$, and the prescribed residue classes.
  - Explanation: Gives a survivor lower average over a whole search bin and an absolute individual upper bound, enabling removal of a small nonaligning subset.
- **claim-euler-weight-truncated-probability** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-stops-euler-probability)): Uniformly over all subsets $E$ of the primes less than $P$, $$\label{eq:stops-euler-lower} \sum_{\substack{d_0\le P^{.95}\\t\mid d_0\Rightarrow t\in E}} \frac{W_E(d_0)}{d_0} \ge e^{-\gamma}(.94-o(1)).$$
  - Explanation: Bounds the truncated Euler-weight sum uniformly over the selected prime subset, including the parity prime.
- **claim-aggregate-comparison-at-stops** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-stopped-comparison)): There is an absolute constant $\eta_0>0$ with the following property. Fix $C_s$ and the regular-box parameters. Take $0<\eta\le\eta_0$, then take $b_0$ sufficiently large in terms of $C_s$, and take any fixed $b_1>b_0$. For all sufficiently small fixed $\xi>0$ and all sufficiently large $z$, the stops of <a href="#def:stops-rule" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:stops-rule">35</a> satisfy $$\label{eq:stops-comparison} \sum_{d\ \mathrm{stopped}} S_d(x(P_d)) \ge \sum_{d\ \mathrm{stopped}}\mu_d P_\mathrm e(r_d,x(P_d)),$$ where $P_d$ denotes the last prime of the tuple and both cutoffs are strict below $P_d$.
  - Explanation: Proves aggregate domination of stopped reference subtrees by exact survivor counts after averaging over the tag prime.
- **claim-hard-endpoint-stopping-coverage** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-hard-stopping)): Fix a compact gap bound $K$, the regular-box parameters, the bounded candidate lists from <a href="#prop:inverse" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:inverse">29</a>, and the exponent $C_s$ from <a href="#cor:hard-endpoints" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:hard-endpoints">33</a>. Fix $0<\eta\le\eta_0$ as in <a href="#prop:stopped-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:stopped-comparison">38</a>. For every $\delta>0$, take $b_0$ sufficiently large in terms of $C_s$ and $K$, then take $b_1/b_0$ sufficiently large in terms of the desired error, and finally take $\xi$ sufficiently small. For these fixed choices, the regular included hard endpoints with $r_d\le K$ having no stopped prefix have harmonic mass at most $$\label{eq:stops-hard-mass} (\delta+o(1))B^{-2}.$$ The eventual $o(1)$ may depend on all the fixed choices. In particular, $b_1$ and $\xi$ are fixed before $z$ tends to infinity.
  - Explanation: Combines correct-tag acquisition and marked even visits to bound the mass of hard compact endpoints without a stopped prefix.
- **claim-modular-hyperbola-completion** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-hyperbola)): Let $H,T_0,T_1$ be positive integers such that $H\ge2$, $T_0$ is squarefree, every prime divisor of $T_0$ divides $H$, and $(T_1,HT_0)=1$. Put $N=HT_0$. Let $C_0\in\mathbb Z$ satisfy $(C_0,H)=1$, and prescribe the residue pattern $$\label{eq:hyperbola-pattern} p_0\in(\mathbb Z/T_0\mathbb Z)^\times,\qquad k_0\in\mathbb Z/T_0\mathbb Z,\qquad p_1\in(\mathbb Z/T_1\mathbb Z)^\times,\qquad m_1\in\mathbb Z/T_1\mathbb Z.$$ The residue classes modulo $1$ have their usual unique interpretation. Let $I,J$ be bounded real intervals of lengths $X,Y_2\ge0$, with either endpoint independently included or excluded. For a positive integer $l$ with $(l,HT_1)=1$, let $C_l$ be the number of integer pairs $(p,m)$ satisfying $$\label{eq:hyperbola-counted-set} \begin{gathered} p\in I,\qquad m\in J,\qquad l\mid p,\\ p\equiv p_0\pmod{T_0},\qquad p\equiv p_1\pmod{T_1},\qquad m\equiv m_1\pmod{T_1},\\ pm\equiv C_0+Hk_0\pmod N. \end{gathered}$$ Here $p$ is an integer variable, without a primality condition. Uniformly in all these data, $$\label{eq:hyperbola-explicit-count} C_l= \frac{XY_2\varphi(N)}{lT_1^2N^2\varphi(T_0)} +O\left( \tau(N)^2\log^2(2N)\sqrt{NT_0} \left(1+\frac{X}{lN}+\frac{Y_2}{N}\right) \right),$$ with an absolute implied constant.* *In particular, suppose that $R\to\infty$ and the data range uniformly over families satisfying $$\label{eq:hyperbola-application-range} R^{4/5}<H\le R\mathcal Z^{11},\qquad X\ll R,\qquad Y_2\le3H,\qquad \mathcal Z,T_0,T_1=R^{o(1)}.$$ Then, uniformly over all the patterns and intervals above and all squarefree $l\le R^{1/10}$ with $(l,HT_1)=1$, $$\label{eq:hyperbola-application-count} C_l= \frac{XY_2\varphi(N)}{lT_1^2N^2\varphi(T_0)} +O\bigl(R^{3/5+o(1)}\bigr).$$ Thus the same assertion holds with error $O(R^{7/10+o(1)})$. Uniformity in the last two assertions means that the upper bounds represented by $R^{o(1)}$ in <a href="#eq:hyperbola-application-range" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:hyperbola-application-range">[eq:hyperbola-application-range]</a> hold uniformly over the family; equivalently, for every $\delta>0$ the error in <a href="#eq:hyperbola-application-count" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:hyperbola-application-count">[eq:hyperbola-application-count]</a> is $O_\delta(R^{3/5+\delta})$ for sufficiently large $R$ in that family. No lower bound for $X/l$ is required.
  - Explanation: Counts integer pairs on a restricted modular hyperbola with multiplicities, endpoint-uniform errors and a specialised uniform application regime.
- **claim-buchstab-limit-normalisation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-elementary-buchstab)): Let $\omega:[1,\infty)\to\mathbb R$ be the continuous function determined by $$u\omega(u)=1\quad(1\le u\le2),\qquad (u\omega(u))'=\omega(u-1)\quad(u>2).$$ Then $\omega(u)\to e^{-\gamma}$ as $u\to\infty$, where $\gamma=\lim_{n\to\infty}(H_n-\log n)$ and $H_n=\sum_{j=1}^n1/j$.
  - Explanation: Determines the Buchstab limit independently of the later positivity argument, closing the normalisation dependency.
- **claim-separated-arcs-large-sieve** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-elementary-large-sieve)): Let $x_1,\ldots,x_R\in\mathbb R/\mathbb Z$ have pairwise circular distance at least $\delta$, where $0<\delta\le1$. Let $J\ge1$ be an integer. If complex coefficients $b_j$ are supported on $J$ consecutive integers, then $$\sum_{r=1}^{R}\left\|\sum_j b_j e(jx_r)\right\|^2 \ll (J+\delta^{-1})\sum_j\|b_j\|^2$$ with an absolute implied constant. Hence <a href="#eq:large-sieve-input" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:large-sieve-input">[eq:large-sieve-input]</a> holds for every real $Q\ge1$.
  - Explanation: Derives the required additive large-sieve order from separated arcs, differentiation and Parseval.
- **claim-affine-bezout-intersections** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-elementary-bezout)): Let $F,G\in\mathbb C[X,Y]$ be nonzero polynomials of respective total degrees $m,n\ge1$, with no common nonconstant factor. Then they have at most $mn$ common zeros in $\mathbb C^2$.
  - Explanation: Bounds distinct affine intersections of coprime complex plane curves through a resultant.
- **claim-progression-prime-upper-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-elementary-brun-titchmarsh)): There are absolute constants $C,J_0>0$ such that, for every integer $q\ge1$, every integer $a$ with $(a,q)=1$, and every real $x$ and $H>0$ with $J=H/q\ge J_0$, $$\#\{x<p\le x+H:p\equiv a\pmod q\} \le C\frac{H}{\varphi(q)\log(H/q)}.$$
  - Explanation: Derives the required progression prime upper bound from the fundamental lemma with a remainder independent of the modulus.

```json
          "source_id": "PM-021.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "claim-fundamental-sieve-estimate",
      "kind": "conditional_result",
      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-fundamental",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal P$ be any subset of the primes at most $v$.",
          "source_id": "PM-021.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-fundamental",
          "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $c,C>0$ and $s_0$ such that for $s\\ge s_0$ the count avoiding all the bad conditions is $$\\label{eq:fundamental-input}\n X\\prod_{p\\in\\mathcal P}(1-g(p))\\bigl(1+O(e^{-cs})\\bigr)\n +O\\!\\left(\n \\sum_{\\substack{l\\le v^s\\\\l\\text{ squarefree}\\\\p\\mid l\\Rightarrow p\\in\\mathcal P}}\n \\tau(l)^C|r_l|\\right).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-021.1"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-lem-fundamental"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-prime-inputs",
        "exact_quote": "The following estimates will be used, with absolute constants unless fixed subscripts indicate a dependence.*\n\n1.  *There is $c>0$ such that $$\\pi(v)=\\mathop{\\mathrm{li}}(v)+O\\!\\left(\\frac v{\\log v}e^{-c\\sqrt{\\log v}}\\right),\n     \\qquad\n     \\prod_{p\\le v}(1-1/p)\n     =\\frac{e^{-\\gamma}}{\\log v}\n       \\bigl(1+O(e^{-c\\sqrt{\\log v}})\\bigr).$$ Decreasing $c$ absorbs harmless powers of $\\log v$.*\n\n2.  *For every fixed $A,M>0$, uniformly for $q\\le(\\log v)^A$ and $(a,q)=1$, $$\\pi(v;q,a)=\\frac{\\mathop{\\mathrm{li}}(v)}{\\varphi(q)}\n                 +O_{A,M}\\!\\left(\\frac v{(\\log v)^M}\\right).$$ The implied constants may be ineffective.*\n\n3.  *For $(a,q)=1$ and $H/q$ exceeding a suitable absolute constant, $$\\#\\{x<p\\le x+H:p\\equiv a\\pmod q\\}\n     \\ll\\frac{H}{\\varphi(q)\\log(H/q)}.$$*\n\n4.  *Uniformly for $v^{9/10}\\le H\\le v$ as $v\\to\\infty$, $$\\#\\{v-H<p\\le v\\}\\ge(1-o(1))\\frac H{\\log v}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-021.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-small-sieve",
        "exact_quote": "Let one forbidden class be given at every prime $p\\le u$, where $u\\to\\infty$. Count the points in a progression segment whose coordinate interval has real length $J$, and whose step is coprime to every prime $p\\le u$. Put $V_{\\mathrm{sm}}(u)=\\prod_{p\\le u}(1-1/p)$. For each fixed $\\eta>0$, uniformly when $J\\ge u^\\eta$, its sifted count $N$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:small-upper}\n N\\ll_\\eta JV_{\\mathrm{sm}}(u).$$ There are absolute $c_1,C_1>0$ and a sufficiently large fixed $v_0$ such that, writing $v=\\log_uJ$, uniformly for $v\\ge v_0$, $$\\label{eq:small-relative}\n \\left|\\frac{N}{JV_{\\mathrm{sm}}(u)}-1\\right|\\le C_1e^{-c_1v}.$$ All estimates are independent of the progression’s initial point, step and forbidden classes.",
        "source_id": "PM-021.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-tree-lower-bound",
        "exact_quote": "The alternating sum of the $N_d$ over all included nodes is a lower bound for $S_1(B)$. More generally, one may stop at any prefix-free collection of included lower nodes, use their exact counts $S_d(b)$, and expand only the other nodes. The resulting expression is still a lower bound for $S_1(B)$. Every included node satisfies $J_d\\ge w^{a_\\star}$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-future-integrals",
        "exact_quote": "The functions $a,c$ are positive and satisfy, with absolute comparison constants, $$\\begin{aligned}\n a(s)+c(s)&=c_0(s),&\n a(s)&\\asymp c_0(s),&\n c(s)&\\asymp c_0(s)\\qquad(s\\ge1),\\label{eq:functions-deficits}\\\\\n s a(s)&=\\int_{s-1}^{\\infty}c(t)\\,dt\n       &&(s\\ge2),\\label{eq:functions-future-a}\\\\\n s c(s)&=\\int_{s-1}^{\\infty}a(t)\\,dt\n       &&(s\\ge1).\\label{eq:functions-future-c}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For $u\\ge2$, $$\\label{eq:functions-dickman-ratio}\n \\frac1{4u^2}\\le\\frac{\\rho(u)}{\\rho(u-1)}\\le\\frac1u .$$ Moreover, for each fixed $\\lambda>0$, there is $U_\\lambda$ such that $$\\label{eq:functions-dickman-logderivative}\n -\\frac{\\rho'(u)}{\\rho(u)}\\ge\\lambda\\qquad(u\\ge U_\\lambda).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-021.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-future-integrals",
          "exact_quote": "The functions $a,c$ are positive and satisfy, with absolute comparison constants, $$\\begin{aligned}\n a(s)+c(s)&=c_0(s),&\n a(s)&\\asymp c_0(s),&\n c(s)&\\asymp c_0(s)\\qquad(s\\ge1),\\label{eq:functions-deficits}\\\\\n s a(s)&=\\int_{s-1}^{\\infty}c(t)\\,dt\n       &&(s\\ge2),\\label{eq:functions-future-a}\\\\\n s c(s)&=\\int_{s-1}^{\\infty}a(t)\\,dt\n       &&(s\\ge1).\\label{eq:functions-future-c}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For $u\\ge2$, $$\\label{eq:functions-dickman-ratio}\n \\frac1{4u^2}\\le\\frac{\\rho(u)}{\\rho(u-1)}\\le\\frac1u .$$ Moreover, for each fixed $\\lambda>0$, there is $U_\\lambda$ such that $$\\label{eq:functions-dickman-logderivative}\n -\\frac{\\rho'(u)}{\\rho(u)}\\ge\\lambda\\qquad(u\\ge U_\\lambda).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-021.1"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-derivative-weights",
        "exact_quote": "The derivative weights are positive and nonincreasing on their indicated ranges. If $i'$ denotes the type opposite to $i$, then $$\\label{eq:functions-derivative-integrals}\n \\phi_i(s)=\\int_{m_i(s)}^{\\infty}W_0(t)\\phi_{i'}(t)\\,dt.$$ This holds also on the specified starting extensions. On a tail, $$\\label{eq:functions-phi-dickman}\n \\phi_\\mathrm e(s)\\asymp\\rho(s-2),\\qquad\n \\phi_\\mathrm o(s)\\asymp\\rho(s-2).$$ There are absolute constants $C,q$ such that the absolute logarithmic derivatives, where they exist, and the ratios $\\phi_{i'}(m_i(s))/\\phi_i(s)$ are at most $C(1+s)^q$. In particular, for $u,v\\le S$ in the indicated domain of the same weight, with $S\\ge3$, $$|\\log\\phi_i(u)-\\log\\phi_i(v)|\n \\le C(1+S)^q|u-v|.$$ For each fixed $\\lambda>0$, the negative logarithmic derivatives of both weights are at least $\\lambda$ on a sufficiently remote tail.",
        "source_id": "PM-021.1"

In particular, for $u,v\\le S$ in the indicated domain of the same weight, with $S\\ge3$, $$|\\log\\phi_i(u)-\\log\\phi_i(v)|\n \\le C(1+S)^q|u-v|.$$ For each fixed $\\lambda>0$, the negative logarithmic derivatives of both weights are at least $\\lambda$ on a sufficiently remote tail.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-regeneration",
        "exact_quote": "The time to first regeneration has an exponential moment uniformly over <a href=\"#eq:functions-start-range\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:functions-start-range\">[eq:functions-start-range]</a>. The cycles strictly after one regeneration through the next inclusive are iid. Their lengths $\\tau$ have a finite exponential moment. Their total log decrements $$G=\\sum_{j=1}^{\\tau}g(s_j)$$ are positive, nonarithmetic, and have a finite exponential moment for some positive parameter. In particular $0<\\mathbb EG<\\infty$. Each complete cycle satisfies $G\\ge g(3)=\\log(4/3)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-021.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-invariant",
        "exact_quote": "On the ordinary even and odd state spaces, respectively, the finite raw invariant densities are $$\\label{eq:functions-invariant-densities}\n \\pi_\\mathrm e(s)=\\phi_\\mathrm e(s)\\quad(s\\ge2),\\qquad\n \\pi_\\mathrm o(s)=(1-s^{-2})\\phi_\\mathrm o(s)\\quad(s\\ge1).$$ Put $$\\beta=\\int_1^3\\pi_\\mathrm o(s)\\,ds=\\frac{4A}{3}.$$ For every nonnegative measurable state function $Q$, $$\\label{eq:functions-cycle-occupation}\n \\mathbb E\\sum_{j=1}^{\\tau}Q(i_j,s_j)\n =\\frac1\\beta\\sum_i\\int Q(i,s)\\pi_i(s)\\,ds.$$ In particular, if $$M_g=\\sum_i\\int\\pi_i(s)g(s)\\,ds,$$ then $\\mathbb EG=M_g/\\beta$, and $$\\label{eq:mg-bound}\n 0<M_g\\le4e^\\gamma.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-021.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-occupation",
        "exact_quote": "For an admissible compact test and starts satisfying <a href=\"#eq:functions-start-range\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:functions-start-range\">[eq:functions-start-range]</a>, $$\\label{eq:functions-occupation-limit}\n \\lim_{v\\to\\infty}\n \\mathbb E_{s_{\\rm in}}\\sum_{n\\ge0}F_{i_n}(v-T_n,s_n)\n =\n \\frac1{M_g}\\sum_i\\int_{\\mathbb R}\\int F_i(u,s)\\pi_i(s)\\,ds\\,du.$$ The convergence is uniform in $s_{\\rm in}\\in[1.99,2.3]$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-band-bounds",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed $h>0$ there is $C_h<\\infty$ with the following properties. For any nonnegative measurable state function $Q$ and any $v\\in\\mathbb R$, a regenerating start satisfies $$\\label{eq:functions-band-from-regeneration}\n \\mathbb E\\sum_{n\\ge1}Q(i_n,s_n)\n        \\mathbf 1_{\\{T_n\\in[v,v+h]\\}}\n \\le C_h\\sum_i\\int Q(i,s)\\pi_i(s)\\,ds.$$ From an even start in <a href=\"#eq:functions-start-range\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:functions-start-range\">[eq:functions-start-range]</a>, the same bound holds for the sum over $n\\ge2$, uniformly in the starting ratio. The density of the first odd state is at most $$C\\,\\mathbf 1_{\\{s\\ge0.98\\}}\\phi_\\mathrm o(s).$$ In particular, uniformly in $r_{\\rm in}>0$, $R>0$, and $\\ell\\in\\{1,2\\}$, $$\\label{eq:functions-inverse-phi-band}\n \\mathbb E\\sum_{n\\ge1}\n \\frac{\\mathbf 1_{\\{R\\le r_n\\le eR,\\ s_n<r_n/\\ell\\}}}\n      {\\phi_{i_n}(s_n)}\n \\ll R+1.$$ Including the starting state in the last sum changes its bound by at most an absolute constant.",

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-marked-visits",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $b_0>0$ and $\\varepsilon>0$. There is a constant $C_\\varepsilon>10$, independent of $b_0$, such that, whenever $b_1\\ge C_\\varepsilon b_0$ is fixed, the following event has probability at least $1-\\varepsilon-o(1)$ as $r_{\\rm in}\\to\\infty$, uniformly in <a href=\"#eq:functions-start-range\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:functions-start-range\">[eq:functions-start-range]</a>. At a regenerating odd state with pre-draw gap in $[10b_0,b_1]$, the next draw is an even arrival satisfying $$s\\in[2.08,2.14],\\qquad x\\in[2b_0,b_1/2].$$ The proof yields the smaller ratio interval $[2.09,2.13]$; thus the stated ratio window has positive slack. The arriving gap exceeds $6b_0$, so, if $6b_0>K$, such a visit precedes every subsequent arrival of gap at most $K$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-high-states",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $K>0$ and $A>0$. The total original harmonic weight of prefixes ending at gap at most $K$ and having some earlier or terminal ratio greater than $S$ is $O_{K,A}(B^{-A})$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-prime-coupling",
        "exact_quote": "Choose sufficiently small absolute positive constants in the following order. For a permitted prime child of a state of type $i$, ratio $s$, and gap $r$, put $t=r/x(p)-1$ and give that child mass $$\\label{eq:prime-tilt}\n q(p\\mid r,s,i)=\\frac1p\\left(\\frac{t+1}{t}\\right)^2\n                    \\frac{\\phi_{i'}(t)}{\\phi_i(s)}.$$ Only children with $x(p)>\\ell$ and $t\\leq S$ are retained. For $\\epsilon_w=\\exp(-c_2\\sqrt{\\log w})$ their total mass is at most $1+\\epsilon_w$. Dividing by $1+\\epsilon_w$ and assigning the remaining mass to a cemetery state defines a killed Markov chain.*\n\n\n\n*This chain can be coupled with the continuous chain <a href=\"#eq:tilted-kernel\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:tilted-kernel\">[eq:tilted-kernel]</a>, killed at the first draw with $x\\leq\\ell$ or $t>S$, so that the probability of failure is at most $\\exp(-c_3\\sqrt{\\log w})$. On success the chains have the same number of arrived-at states and matching exits. At corresponding states, $$\\label{eq:prime-coupling-errors}\n |s-\\widetilde s|\\leq h,\\qquad\n \\left|\\log\\frac r{\\widetilde r}\\right|\\leq C N h,\\qquad\n h=\\frac1{\\lceil\\exp(c_4\\sqrt{\\log w})\\rceil}.$$ All statements are uniform in $s_{\\mathrm{st}}\\in[1.99,2.3]$ and in the endpoint conventions stated above. For a length-$m$ retained prefix, if $\\mathbb P_w(\\mathfrak p)$ denotes its probability in the killed chain, then the exact weight identity is $$\\label{eq:prime-weight}\n h(\\mathfrak p)=\\mathbb P_w(\\mathfrak p)(1+\\epsilon_w)^m\n \\left(\\frac{r(\\mathfrak p)}{r_{\\mathrm{st}}}\\right)^2\n \\frac{\\phi_\\mathrm e(s_{\\mathrm{st}})}{\\phi_i(s(\\mathfrak p))},\n \\qquad (1+\\epsilon_w)^m=1+o(1).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-021.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-prime-coupling",
          "exact_quote": "Choose sufficiently small absolute positive constants in the following order. For $\\epsilon_w=\\exp(-c_2\\sqrt{\\log w})$ their total mass is at most $1+\\epsilon_w$. For a length-$m$ retained prefix, if $\\mathbb P_w(\\mathfrak p)$ denotes its probability in the killed chain, then the exact weight identity is $$\\label{eq:prime-weight}\n h(\\mathfrak p)=\\mathbb P_w(\\mathfrak p)(1+\\epsilon_w)^m\n \\left(\\frac{r(\\mathfrak p)}{r_{\\mathrm{st}}}\\right)^2\n \\frac{\\phi_\\mathrm e(s_{\\mathrm{st}})}{\\phi_i(s(\\mathfrak p))},\n \\qquad (1+\\epsilon_w)^m=1+o(1).$$",

      "exact_statement": "Choose sufficiently small absolute positive constants in the following order. Dividing by $1+\\epsilon_w$ and assigning the remaining mass to a cemetery state defines a killed Markov chain.*\n\n\n\n*This chain can be coupled with the continuous chain <a href=\"#eq:tilted-kernel\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:tilted-kernel\">[eq:tilted-kernel]</a>, killed at the first draw with $x\\leq\\ell$ or $t>S$, so that the probability of failure is at most $\\exp(-c_3\\sqrt{\\log w})$. On success the chains have the same number of arrived-at states and matching exits. At corresponding states, $$\\label{eq:prime-coupling-errors}\n |s-\\widetilde s|\\leq h,\\qquad\n \\left|\\log\\frac r{\\widetilde r}\\right|\\leq C N h,\\qquad\n h=\\frac1{\\lceil\\exp(c_4\\sqrt{\\log w})\\rceil}.$$ All statements are uniform in $s_{\\mathrm{st}}\\in[1.99,2.3]$ and in the endpoint conventions stated above. For a length-$m$ retained prefix, if $\\mathbb P_w(\\mathfrak p)$ denotes its probability in the killed chain, then the exact weight identity is $$\\label{eq:prime-weight}\n h(\\mathfrak p)=\\mathbb P_w(\\mathfrak p)(1+\\epsilon_w)^m\n \\left(\\frac{r(\\mathfrak p)}{r_{\\mathrm{st}}}\\right)^2\n \\frac{\\phi_\\mathrm e(s_{\\mathrm{st}})}{\\phi_i(s(\\mathfrak p))},\n \\qquad (1+\\epsilon_w)^m=1+o(1).$$",
      "explanation": "Defines a killed prime chain, couples it to the continuous tilted chain and retains an exact telescoping conversion to harmonic weights.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",

        "exact_quote": "For the killed chain of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:prime-coupling\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:prime-coupling\">17</a>, the probability of a nonexit bin with left endpoint $t$ at step $2\\leq n\\leq N$ is bounded, with an absolute constant, by $$\\label{eq:prime-marginals}\n \\begin{cases}\n  C h\\phi_\\mathrm e(t),&n\\ \\hbox{even},\\\\[2mm]\n  C h\\phi_\\mathrm o(t)\\bigl(1-t^{-2}+3hW_0(t)\\bigr),&n\\ \\hbox{odd}.\n \\end{cases}$$ At step $1$ the bound is $C hW_0(t)\\phi_\\mathrm o(t)$, on the slightly extended range $t\\geq0.98$. The constants are uniform in the starting ratios and in $w$ sufficiently large.",

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-prime-occupation",
        "exact_quote": "The following assertions hold uniformly for $s_{\\mathrm{st}}\\in[1.99,2.3]$ and $\\ell\\in\\{1,2\\}$.*\n\n1.  *For fixed $K$, the original harmonic mass of prefixes with terminal gap at most $K$ is $O_K(B^{-2})$.*\n\n\n2.  *Consider any selection of such compact-ending prefixes whose low-state histories imply an event $\\mathcal A$ in the killed prime chain. Their total weight is at most $$\\label{eq:prime-compact-event}\n     C_K B^{-2}\\mathbb P_w(\\mathcal A)+O_{K,A}(B^{-A})$$ for every fixed $A>0$. One may take $\\mathcal A$ to be the event that at least one selected compact prefix occurs.*\n\n\n3.  *For every fixed $c'>0$, $$\\label{eq:prime-tail}\n     \\lim_{K\\to\\infty}\\limsup_{w\\to\\infty}\n     B^2\\sum_{\\substack{\\mathfrak p\\ \\mathrm{standard}\\\\r(\\mathfrak p)>K}}\n           h(\\mathfrak p)e^{-c'r(\\mathfrak p)}=0.$$ The same compact and tail assertions hold for continuous original harmonic weights.*\n\n\n4.  *Let $H_i(r,s)$ be bounded continuous functions supported in a fixed compact positive gap interval and a fixed compact ratio interval. Fixed interval boundaries and the indicator $s<r/\\ell$ are also permitted, with one-sided continuous extension before imposing that indicator, as in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:occupation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:occupation\">11</a>. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n    \\label{eq:prime-occupation}\n     &\\frac{r_{\\mathrm{st}}^2}{\\phi_\\mathrm e(s_{\\mathrm{st}})}\n       \\sum_{\\mathfrak p}h(\\mathfrak p)\n            H_{i(\\mathfrak p)}(r(\\mathfrak p),s(\\mathfrak p))\\notag\\\\\n     &\\hspace{8mm}\\longrightarrow\n     \\frac1{M_g}\\sum_{i\\in\\{\\mathrm e,\\mathrm o\\}}\n       \\int_0^\\infty\\int\n        \\frac{r^2\\pi_i(s)}{\\phi_i(s)}H_i(r,s)\n           \\mathbf 1_{s<r/\\ell}\\,ds\\,\\frac{dr}{r},\n    \\end{aligned}$$ where the inner domains are $s\\geq2$ for $i=\\mathrm e$ and $s\\geq1$ for $i=\\mathrm o$. The convergence is uniform in the starting ratios.",
        "source_id": "PM-021.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-local-reference",
        "exact_quote": "For the continuous reference polynomial, $$\\label{eq:ref-local-cont}\n Q_\\mathrm e(r,b)=\\frac{f(r/b)}b+O(b^{-2})\n \\qquad\\left(b\\longrightarrow\\infty,\\quad2\\leq r/b\\leq2.22\\right),$$ uniformly in the displayed ratios.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-edge-witness",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $K$ and $0<\\varepsilon<1$. One can choose a fixed $K_*>K+10$ such that, with $$H_e=\\lceil 2K_*+10\\rceil,\\qquad\n \\delta=\\frac{\\varepsilon}{4H_e},$$ the following holds for all sufficiently large $z$, uniformly in the prescribed residue classes. If an included endpoint $d$ has $r_d\\le K$ and $|N_d/\\mu_d-1|>\\varepsilon$, then one of its last $H_e$ edges, with parent $d'$ and child prime $u$, satisfies $$\\label{eq:boxes-witness}\n \\left|N_{d'u}-\\frac{N_{d'}}u\\right|\n       >\\delta\\frac{\\mu_{d'}}u,\n \\qquad\n x(u),\\ \\log_w J_{d'}\\le 2K_*+3,\n \\qquad\n \\log_w J_{d'u}\\ge a_\\star.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-021.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-edge-witness",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $K$ and $0<\\varepsilon<1$. If an included endpoint $d$ has $r_d\\le K$ and $|N_d/\\mu_d-1|>\\varepsilon$, then one of its last $H_e$ edges, with parent $d'$ and child prime $u$, satisfies $$\\label{eq:boxes-witness}\n \\left|N_{d'u}-\\frac{N_{d'}}u\\right|\n       >\\delta\\frac{\\mu_{d'}}u,\n \\qquad\n x(u),\\ \\log_w J_{d'}\\le 2K_*+3,\n \\qquad\n \\log_w J_{d'u}\\ge a_\\star.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-021.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix $K$ and $0<\\varepsilon<1$. One can choose a fixed $K_*>K+10$ such that, with $$H_e=\\lceil 2K_*+10\\rceil,\\qquad\n \\delta=\\frac{\\varepsilon}{4H_e},$$ the following holds for all sufficiently large $z$, uniformly in the prescribed residue classes. If an included endpoint $d$ has $r_d\\le K$ and $|N_d/\\mu_d-1|>\\varepsilon$, then one of its last $H_e$ edges, with parent $d'$ and child prime $u$, satisfies $$\\label{eq:boxes-witness}\n \\left|N_{d'u}-\\frac{N_{d'}}u\\right|\n       >\\delta\\frac{\\mu_{d'}}u,\n \\qquad\n x(u),\\ \\log_w J_{d'}\\le 2K_*+3,\n \\qquad\n \\log_w J_{d'u}\\ge a_\\star.$$",
      "explanation": "Locates a recent discrepant edge whenever a compact endpoint has a large relative counting error.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",

        }

      "id": "claim-recent-discrepant-edge",
      "kind": "conditional_result",
      "quantifiers": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-regular-boxes",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $K$ and $K_*>K+10$. For every $\\vartheta>0$ there are fixed constants $C_{\\rm len}$ and $\\alpha_{\\min}>0$, followed by constants $\\xi_0>0$ and $C_{\\rm th}<\\infty$, with the following properties. They are chosen before $z$ tends to infinity, and $C_{\\rm th}$ is independent of $0<\\xi\\le\\xi_0$. Put $$\\label{eq:boxes-delta}\n \\Delta=2C_{\\rm len}\\xi.$$ For each fixed $0<\\xi\\le\\xi_0$ and sufficiently large $z$, there is a family $\\mathcal G$ of regular included endpoints with $r_d\\le K$ such that $$\\label{eq:boxes-exception}\n \\mathfrak m\\bigl(\\{d\\text{ included}:r_d\\le K\\}\\setminus\\mathcal G\\bigr)\n \\le \\frac{\\vartheta+C_{\\rm th}\\Delta+o_\\xi(1)}{B^2}.$$ The term $o_\\xi(1)$ tends to zero after all fixed parameters, including $\\xi$, have been chosen. The statement is uniform in the prescribed residue classes. The regular endpoints and their covering full boxes have these additional properties.*\n\n1.  *Every regular endpoint has length at most $C_{\\rm len}\\log B$ and has an isolated position. Every used search bin, including its isolated bin, has multiplicity one. Every position satisfying $$x_j\\le2K_*+3,\\qquad r_{j-1}\\le2K_*+5$$ also has multiplicity one. The isolated position precedes every edge supplied by Lemma <a href=\"#lem:edge-witness\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:edge-witness\">25</a>.*\n\n\n\n2.  *For every nonempty even prefix of a regular endpoint, $$\\label{eq:boxes-threshold-clearance}\n     r_j-\\mathcal H(x_j)>10\\Delta.$$ Moving prime choices anywhere within their fixed bins changes any prefix gap by at most $\\Delta$, and changes a threshold expression $r_j-\\mathcal H(x_j)$ by at most $\\Delta+2h$. In particular the representative gaps and exponents of every regular box satisfy $$\\label{eq:boxes-representative-safety}\n     r_j^{\\rm rep}\\ge \\mathcal H(x_j^{\\rm rep})+3\\Delta\n     \\quad\\text{at every nonempty even prefix}.$$*\n\n\n3.  *Let $\\mathfrak B$ be the distinct full boxes containing at least one regular endpoint. There is a constant $C_K^{\\rm box}$, depending only on a fixed enlargement of the endpoint compact range and the allowed starting ratios, such that $$\\label{eq:boxes-expanded-mass}\n     \\sum_{\\mathcal B\\in\\mathfrak B}\\mathfrak m(\\mathcal B)\n           \\le\\frac{C_K^{\\rm box}}{B^2}.$$ In particular this constant is independent of $\\xi$, $\\vartheta$, and the total number of boxes. Every tuple in these boxes is a standard path from initial gap $r_0+1$, ends at gap at most $K+2$ for that starting gap, and has its actual final $\\log_wJ\\ge3a_\\star/4$.*\n\n\n\n\n4.  *In a regular box, declare an edge a candidate if it is among the last $H_e=\\lceil2K_*+10\\rceil$ positions, is after the isolated position, has multiplicity one, and can satisfy the size bounds in <a href=\"#eq:boxes-witness\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:boxes-witness\">[eq:boxes-witness]</a> for some tuple in the box. There are at most $H_e$ candidates, and every witness for a regular endpoint is among them. Throughout the whole box of each candidate, writing its parent as $d'=pq$ and its prime as $u$, one has $$\\label{eq:boxes-candidate-slack}\n     \\log_w\\frac{Y}{pq}\\le2K_*+4,\n     \\qquad\n     \\log_w\\frac{Y}{pqu}\\ge\\frac{3a_\\star}{4}.$$ The bins for $p$ and $u$ have lower endpoints $R$ and $U$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:boxes-prime-ranges}\n     z^{\\alpha_{\\min}}\\le R\\le z^{\\alpha_{\\max}},\n     \\qquad w\\le U\\le w^{2K_*+4}.$$ The choices of $p$, $q$, $u$, and the descendants after this edge are independent under the full-box measure. All their prime factors are disjoint. If $S_q$ is the product of the bin upper endpoints for the factors of $q$, with their specified multiplicities, then $$\\label{eq:boxes-sq}\n     1\\le\\frac{S_q}{q}\\le(1+\\xi)^{C_{\\rm len}\\log B}.$$*\n\n5.  *Every regular box has at least $c_7\\log w$ search positions, for an absolute $c_7>0$ and sufficiently large $z$. These positions have distinct bins. All its nonsearch positions follow all its search positions.",
        "source_id": "PM-021.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-inverse",
        "exact_quote": "Fix the parameters in <a href=\"#eq:inverse-box-ranges\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:inverse-box-ranges\">[eq:inverse-box-ranges]</a>– <a href=\"#eq:inverse-q-range\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:inverse-q-range\">[eq:inverse-q-range]</a>, the witnessing threshold $\\delta>0$, and $0<\\sigma<1$. There are constants $c_p>0$, a list-size bound $F<\\infty$, and $\\xi_{\\rm inv}>0$, independent of the eventual choice of fixed $0<\\xi\\le\\xi_{\\rm inv}$, with the following property for all sufficiently large $z$.*\n\n\n\n\n*The full candidate box has a list $\\mathcal A$ of at most $F$ reduced rationals $A/D$ satisfying $$D\\le R\\mathcal Z^{10},\\qquad\n |A|\\le S_qR\\mathcal Z^{10},\\qquad\n \\#\\{p\\in\\mathcal P:p\\text{ aligns with }A/D\\}\\ge c_p n_p .\n \\label{eq:inverse-list}$$ Outside a set of normalized harmonic box measure at most $\\sigma$, every occurrence of $$\\left|N_{pqu}-\\frac{N_{pq}}u\\right|\n       >\\delta\\,\\frac{YV_0}{pqu}\n \\label{eq:inverse-bad-edge}$$ has a rational $A/D\\in\\mathcal A$ for which $$p\\text{ aligns with }A/D,\n \\qquad D q_E(A/D)\\le R\\mathcal Z^{10}.\n \\label{eq:inverse-structure}$$ The exceptional measure is relative to the whole box, rather than to the subset on which <a href=\"#eq:inverse-bad-edge\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:inverse-bad-edge\">[eq:inverse-bad-edge]</a> holds.",
        "source_id": "PM-021.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-inverse",
          "exact_quote": "Fix the parameters in <a href=\"#eq:inverse-box-ranges\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:inverse-box-ranges\">[eq:inverse-box-ranges]</a>– <a href=\"#eq:inverse-q-range\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:inverse-q-range\">[eq:inverse-q-range]</a>, the witnessing threshold $\\delta>0$, and $0<\\sigma<1$. There are constants $c_p>0$, a list-size bound $F<\\infty$, and $\\xi_{\\rm inv}>0$, independent of the eventual choice of fixed $0<\\xi\\le\\xi_{\\rm inv}$, with the following property for all sufficiently large $z$.*\n\n\n\n\n*The full candidate box has a list $\\mathcal A$ of at most $F$ reduced rationals $A/D$ satisfying $$D\\le R\\mathcal Z^{10},\\qquad\n |A|\\le S_qR\\mathcal Z^{10},\\qquad\n \\#\\{p\\in\\mathcal P:p\\text{ aligns with }A/D\\}\\ge c_p n_p .\n \\label{eq:inverse-list}$$ Outside a set of normalized harmonic box measure at most $\\sigma$, every occurrence of $$\\left|N_{pqu}-\\frac{N_{pq}}u\\right|\n       >\\delta\\,\\frac{YV_0}{pqu}\n \\label{eq:inverse-bad-edge}$$ has a rational $A/D\\in\\mathcal A$ for which $$p\\text{ aligns with }A/D,\n \\qquad D q_E(A/D)\\le R\\mathcal Z^{10}.\n \\label{eq:inverse-structure}$$ The exceptional measure is relative to the whole box, rather than to the subset on which <a href=\"#eq:inverse-bad-edge\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:inverse-bad-edge\">[eq:inverse-bad-edge]</a> holds.",
          "source_id": "PM-021.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Fix the parameters in <a href=\"#eq:inverse-box-ranges\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:inverse-box-ranges\">[eq:inverse-box-ranges]</a>– <a href=\"#eq:inverse-q-range\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:inverse-q-range\">[eq:inverse-q-range]</a>, the witnessing threshold $\\delta>0$, and $0<\\sigma<1$. There are constants $c_p>0$, a list-size bound $F<\\infty$, and $\\xi_{\\rm inv}>0$, independent of the eventual choice of fixed $0<\\xi\\le\\xi_{\\rm inv}$, with the following property for all sufficiently large $z$.*\n\n\n\n\n*The full candidate box has a list $\\mathcal A$ of at most $F$ reduced rationals $A/D$ satisfying $$D\\le R\\mathcal Z^{10},\\qquad\n |A|\\le S_qR\\mathcal Z^{10},\\qquad\n \\#\\{p\\in\\mathcal P:p\\text{ aligns with }A/D\\}\\ge c_p n_p .\n \\label{eq:inverse-list}$$ Outside a set of normalized harmonic box measure at most $\\sigma$, every occurrence of $$\\left|N_{pqu}-\\frac{N_{pq}}u\\right|\n       >\\delta\\,\\frac{YV_0}{pqu}\n \\label{eq:inverse-bad-edge}$$ has a rational $A/D\\in\\mathcal A$ for which $$p\\text{ aligns with }A/D,\n \\qquad D q_E(A/D)\\le R\\mathcal Z^{10}.\n \\label{eq:inverse-structure}$$ The exceptional measure is relative to the whole box, rather than to the subset on which <a href=\"#eq:inverse-bad-edge\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:inverse-bad-edge\">[eq:inverse-bad-edge]</a> holds.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-nonlinear-points",
        "exact_quote": "The number of lattice points in $[0,S]^2$ lying on at least one nonlinear irreducible component of $P=0$, with irreducibility taken over $\\mathbb C$, is $$O\\!\\left(D_0^4(1+S^{2/3})\\right).$$ The implied constant is absolute, independently of the coefficients of $P$.",
        "source_id": "PM-021.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-nonlinear-points",
          "exact_quote": "Let $P\\in\\mathbb R[X_1,X_2]$ be nonzero and of degree at most $D_0\\ge1$, and let $S\\ge1$. The number of lattice points in $[0,S]^2$ lying on at least one nonlinear irreducible component of $P=0$, with irreducibility taken over $\\mathbb C$, is $$O\\!\\left(D_0^4(1+S^{2/3})\\right).$$ The implied constant is absolute, independently of the coefficients of $P$. No bound is asserted for points lying only on line components.",
          "source_id": "PM-021.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $P\\in\\mathbb R[X_1,X_2]$ be nonzero and of degree at most $D_0\\ge1$, and let $S\\ge1$. The number of lattice points in $[0,S]^2$ lying on at least one nonlinear irreducible component of $P=0$, with irreducibility taken over $\\mathbb C$, is $$O\\!\\left(D_0^4(1+S^{2/3})\\right).$$ The implied constant is absolute, independently of the coefficients of $P$. No bound is asserted for points lying only on line components.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-random-variance",
        "exact_quote": "Choose, independently at each prime $t\\le w$, one uniformly distributed forbidden residue class. Let $S$ count the integers in an interval $\\mathcal J$ of cardinality $J$ that avoid all these classes. For every fixed $\\delta>0$, uniformly for $J\\ge w^\\delta$, $$\\mathbb ES=JV_0,\\qquad\n \\mathbb E(S-JV_0)^2=o((JV_0)^2)\n \\quad(w\\longrightarrow\\infty).$$ The assertion has the following stable version. Let $\\mu$ be a finite positive measure carrying indicators $I_j$, $j\\in\\mathcal J$, and let $S=\\sum_j I_j$. Suppose, with an absolute fixed implied constant, $$\\mu(\\Omega)=1+O(\\zeta),\\qquad\n \\int I_j\\,d\\mu=V_0(1+O(\\zeta)),$$ and, for distinct $i,j$, the joint probability has the upper bound $$\\int I_iI_j\\,d\\mu\n \\le(1+O(\\zeta))\\,\\mathbb P_{\\rm ind}(I_i=I_j=1).$$ Here $\\mathbb P_{\\rm ind}$ denotes the independent forbidden-class model in the first part of the lemma. A zero probability on the right requires exact zero on the left. For $0\\le\\zeta\\le1/2$, $$\\int(S-JV_0)^2\\,d\\mu\n \\ll\\bigl(\\zeta+o(1)\\bigr)(JV_0)^2,$$ uniformly in the same range of $J$.",
        "source_id": "PM-021.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-random-variance",
          "exact_quote": "Choose, independently at each prime $t\\le w$, one uniformly distributed forbidden residue class. Let $S$ count the integers in an interval $\\mathcal J$ of cardinality $J$ that avoid all these classes. For every fixed $\\delta>0$, uniformly for $J\\ge w^\\delta$, $$\\mathbb ES=JV_0,\\qquad\n \\mathbb E(S-JV_0)^2=o((JV_0)^2)\n \\quad(w\\longrightarrow\\infty).$$ The assertion has the following stable version. Let $\\mu$ be a finite positive measure carrying indicators $I_j$, $j\\in\\mathcal J$, and let $S=\\sum_j I_j$. Suppose, with an absolute fixed implied constant, $$\\mu(\\Omega)=1+O(\\zeta),\\qquad\n \\int I_j\\,d\\mu=V_0(1+O(\\zeta)),$$ and, for distinct $i,j$, the joint probability has the upper bound $$\\int I_iI_j\\,d\\mu\n \\le(1+O(\\zeta))\\,\\mathbb P_{\\rm ind}(I_i=I_j=1).$$ Here $\\mathbb P_{\\rm ind}$ denotes the independent forbidden-class model in the first part of the lemma. A zero probability on the right requires exact zero on the left. For $0\\le\\zeta\\le1/2$, $$\\int(S-JV_0)^2\\,d\\mu\n \\ll\\bigl(\\zeta+o(1)\\bigr)(JV_0)^2,$$ uniformly in the same range of $J$.",
          "source_id": "PM-021.1"
        }
      ],

        "exact_quote": "Fix the compact-box parameters, the inverse-estimate parameters and a relative-error threshold $\\varepsilon>0$.         "anchor": "stmt-cor-hard-endpoints",
        "exact_quote": "Fix the compact range and $\\varepsilon>0$. For every prescribed positive $\\rho$, the inverse exception parameter and then the variance exception parameter can be chosen so that, for a fixed $C_s$ independent of sufficiently small fixed $\\xi$, the following holds for all sufficiently large $z$. Consider the actual included regular compact endpoints covered by the full boxes of <a href=\"#prop:regular-boxes\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:regular-boxes\">26</a>. Except for a set of these endpoints of total harmonic mass at most $\\rho B^{-2}$, every such bad endpoint $$|N_d/\\mu_d-1|>\\varepsilon$$ has a witnessing candidate and a rational on that candidate’s fixed list for which the parent is structured, the isolated prime aligns, and $$\\label{eq:variance-hard}\n Dq_{f,E}\\le w^{C_s},\n \\qquad |C_0|/R\\le w^{C_s}.$$ For each such endpoint all its prime factors with exponent $x(t)>C_s$ align to this same rational, including the isolated prime. The rational satisfies the global bounds $$\\label{eq:variance-global-height}\n D\\le w^{C_s},\n \\qquad |A|\\le Yw^{C_s+2}.$$ The enlarged full boxes are used only to dominate measure: the assertion concerns the actual included endpoints, rather than every tuple in those enlarged boxes. The exceptional mass can be bounded before choosing $\\xi$; only the eventual threshold on $z$ is allowed to depend on fixed $\\xi$.",
        "source_id": "PM-021.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-stops-aligned-average",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $C_s$. Take $b_0$ sufficiently large in terms of $C_s$, and let $b_1>b_0$ be fixed. For every sufficiently small fixed $\\xi>0$, the following holds for all sufficiently large $z$. Let $A/D$ be eligible in the sense of <a href=\"#eq:stops-eligible\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:stops-eligible\">[eq:stops-eligible]</a>, let $P=w^b$ be a prime with $b\\in[b_0-1,b_1]$, and let $q'$ be a squarefree product of primes at most $z$ whose least prime factor is $P$ and all of whose prime factors align to $A/D$. Let $\\mathcal P$ be a search bin, none of whose primes divides $q'$. Suppose that, for every $p\\in\\mathcal P$, $$\\label{eq:stops-length-exponents}\n J_p=\\frac{Y}{pq'},\\qquad 2.03\\le\\log_PJ_p\\le2.19.$$ Define $T_p$ to count the integers $1\\le n\\le Y$ satisfying $Dn\\equiv A\\pmod{pq'}$ and avoiding the prescribed classes at every prime $t<P$. Then $$\\label{eq:stops-all-primes-lower}\n \\sum_{p\\in\\mathcal P}T_p\n \\ge .39V_{\\rm all}(P)\\sum_{p\\in\\mathcal P}J_p.$$ There is also an absolute constant $C_U$, independent of $C_s,b_0,b_1$ and of sufficiently small fixed $\\xi$, such that $$\\label{eq:stops-individual-upper}\n T_p\\le C_UJ_pV_{\\rm all}(P)\\qquad(p\\in\\mathcal P).$$ Both estimates are uniform in the eligible rational, the bin, the product $q'$, and the prescribed residue classes.",
        "source_id": "PM-021.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-stopped-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "There is an absolute constant $\\eta_0>0$ with the following property. Fix $C_s$ and the regular-box parameters. For all sufficiently small fixed $\\xi>0$ and all sufficiently large $z$, the stops of <a href=\"#def:stops-rule\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:stops-rule\">35</a> satisfy $$\\label{eq:stops-comparison}\n \\sum_{d\\ \\mathrm{stopped}} S_d(x(P_d))\n \\ge\n \\sum_{d\\ \\mathrm{stopped}}\\mu_d P_\\mathrm e(r_d,x(P_d)),$$ where $P_d$ denotes the last prime of the tuple and both cutoffs are strict below $P_d$.",

      "exact_statement": "There is an absolute constant $\\eta_0>0$ with the following property. Fix $C_s$ and the regular-box parameters.         "exact_quote": "Fix a compact gap bound $K$, the regular-box parameters, the bounded candidate lists from <a href=\"#prop:inverse\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:inverse\">29</a>, and the exponent $C_s$ from <a href=\"#cor:hard-endpoints\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"cor:hard-endpoints\">33</a>. Fix $0<\\eta\\le\\eta_0$ as in <a href=\"#prop:stopped-comparison\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:stopped-comparison\">38</a>. For every $\\delta>0$, take $b_0$ sufficiently large in terms of $C_s$ and $K$, then take $b_1/b_0$ sufficiently large in terms of the desired error, and finally take $\\xi$ sufficiently small. For these fixed choices, the regular included hard endpoints with $r_d\\le K$ having no stopped prefix have harmonic mass at most $$\\label{eq:stops-hard-mass}\n (\\delta+o(1))B^{-2}.$$ The eventual $o(1)$ may depend on all the fixed choices.
```
