      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-hyperbola",
        "exact_quote": "Let $H,T_0,T_1$ be positive integers such that $H\\ge2$, $T_0$ is squarefree, every prime divisor of $T_0$ divides $H$, and $(T_1,HT_0)=1$. Put $N=HT_0$. Let $C_0\\in\\mathbb Z$ satisfy $(C_0,H)=1$, and prescribe the residue pattern $$\\label{eq:hyperbola-pattern}\n p_0\\in(\\mathbb Z/T_0\\mathbb Z)^\\times,\\qquad k_0\\in\\mathbb Z/T_0\\mathbb Z,\\qquad\n p_1\\in(\\mathbb Z/T_1\\mathbb Z)^\\times,\\qquad m_1\\in\\mathbb Z/T_1\\mathbb Z.$$ The residue classes modulo $1$ have their usual unique interpretation. Let $I,J$ be bounded real intervals of lengths $X,Y_2\\ge0$, with either endpoint independently included or excluded. For a positive integer $l$ with $(l,HT_1)=1$, let $C_l$ be the number of integer pairs $(p,m)$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:hyperbola-counted-set}\n\\begin{gathered}\n p\\in I,\\qquad m\\in J,\\qquad l\\mid p,\\\\\n p\\equiv p_0\\pmod{T_0},\\qquad\n p\\equiv p_1\\pmod{T_1},\\qquad\n m\\equiv m_1\\pmod{T_1},\\\\\n pm\\equiv C_0+Hk_0\\pmod N.\n\\end{gathered}$$ Here $p$ is an integer variable, without a primality condition. Uniformly in all these data, $$\\label{eq:hyperbola-explicit-count}\n C_l=\n \\frac{XY_2\\varphi(N)}{lT_1^2N^2\\varphi(T_0)}\n +O\\left(\n \\tau(N)^2\\log^2(2N)\\sqrt{NT_0}\n \\left(1+\\frac{X}{lN}+\\frac{Y_2}{N}\\right)\n \\right),$$ with an absolute implied constant.*\n\n\n\n*In particular, suppose that $R\\to\\infty$ and the data range uniformly over families satisfying $$\\label{eq:hyperbola-application-range}\n R^{4/5}<H\\le R\\mathcal Z^{11},\\qquad\n X\\ll R,\\qquad Y_2\\le3H,\\qquad\n \\mathcal Z,T_0,T_1=R^{o(1)}.$$ Then, uniformly over all the patterns and intervals above and all squarefree $l\\le R^{1/10}$ with $(l,HT_1)=1$, $$\\label{eq:hyperbola-application-count}\n C_l=\n \\frac{XY_2\\varphi(N)}{lT_1^2N^2\\varphi(T_0)}\n +O\\bigl(R^{3/5+o(1)}\\bigr).$$ Thus the same assertion holds with error $O(R^{7/10+o(1)})$. Uniformity in the last two assertions means that the upper bounds represented by $R^{o(1)}$ in <a href=\"#eq:hyperbola-application-range\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:hyperbola-application-range\">[eq:hyperbola-application-range]</a> hold uniformly over the family; equivalently, for every $\\delta>0$ the error in <a href=\"#eq:hyperbola-application-count\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:hyperbola-application-count\">[eq:hyperbola-application-count]</a> is $O_\\delta(R^{3/5+\\delta})$ for sufficiently large $R$ in that family. No lower bound for $X/l$ is required.",
        "source_id": "PM-021.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-elementary-buchstab",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\omega:[1,\\infty)\\to\\mathbb R$ be the continuous function determined by $$u\\omega(u)=1\\quad(1\\le u\\le2),\\qquad\n (u\\omega(u))'=\\omega(u-1)\\quad(u>2).$$ Then $\\omega(u)\\to e^{-\\gamma}$ as $u\\to\\infty$, where $\\gamma=\\lim_{n\\to\\infty}(H_n-\\log n)$ and $H_n=\\sum_{j=1}^n1/j$.",
        "source_id": "PM-021.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-elementary-large-sieve",
        "exact_quote": "Let $x_1,\\ldots,x_R\\in\\mathbb R/\\mathbb Z$ have pairwise circular distance at least $\\delta$, where $0<\\delta\\le1$. Let $J\\ge1$ be an integer. If complex coefficients $b_j$ are supported on $J$ consecutive integers, then $$\\sum_{r=1}^{R}\\left|\\sum_j b_j e(jx_r)\\right|^2\n   \\ll (J+\\delta^{-1})\\sum_j|b_j|^2$$ with an absolute implied constant. Hence <a href=\"#eq:large-sieve-input\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:large-sieve-input\">[eq:large-sieve-input]</a> holds for every real $Q\\ge1$.",
        "source_id": "PM-021.1"
      },

          "exact_quote": "Let $x_1,\\ldots,x_R\\in\\mathbb R/\\mathbb Z$ have pairwise circular distance at least $\\delta$, where $0<\\delta\\le1$. Let $J\\ge1$ be an integer.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-elementary-brun-titchmarsh",
        "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $C,J_0>0$ such that, for every integer $q\\ge1$, every integer $a$ with $(a,q)=1$, and every real $x$ and $H>0$ with $J=H/q\\ge J_0$, $$\\#\\{x<p\\le x+H:p\\equiv a\\pmod q\\}\n   \\le C\\frac{H}{\\varphi(q)\\log(H/q)}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-021.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-021.1"
        }
      ],

<!-- PM:END pm.claims.v2 -->

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Jacobsthal function** ([source](#pm-h-2)): For a positive integer $n$, let $j(n)$ be the least positive integer $m$ such that every interval of $m$ consecutive integers contains an integer coprime to $n$.
- **Distinct-prime count and uniform Jacobsthal maximum** ([source](#pm-h-2)): Write $\omega(n)$ for the number of distinct prime divisors of $n$, with $\omega(1)=0$, and put $$h(k)=\sup_{\substack{n\ge1\\\omega(n)\le k}}j(n),\qquad k\ge1.$$
- **Sieve parameter scales** ([source](#eq:parameters)): For sufficiently large real $z$, set $$\label{eq:parameters} \begin{gathered} L=\log z,\qquad Y=\left\lfloor\frac{z^2}{L^2}\right\rfloor, \qquad w=\frac{L}{(\log L)^2},\qquad B=\frac{L}{\log w},\\ V_0=\prod_{p\le w}\left(1-\frac1p\right). \end{gathered}$$
- **Decreasing-prime node** ([source](#sec:tree)): A node is a finite tuple of strictly decreasing primes in $(w,z]$.
- **Node product** ([source](#sec:tree)): Its product is denoted by $d$, with $d=1$ for the empty tuple.
- **Parity types and admission rule** ([source](#eq:admission)): Nodes of even length are called lower nodes, and nodes of odd length are called upper nodes. From a lower node include every available child. From an upper node include a child with exponent $x=x(p)$ exactly when $$\label{eq:admission} r-x\ge \mathcal H(x),\qquad \mathcal H(x)=\max(2x,x+2),$$
- **Harmonic path weight** ([source](#pm-h-10)): A path’s *harmonic weight* is the reciprocal product of its chosen primes.
- **Linear-sieve delay functions** ([source](#eq:functions-starting-extension)): Define the continuous delay functions $f,F$ by $$f(s)=0\quad(0\le s\le2),\qquad sF(s)=A\quad(1\le s\le3),$$ and $$(sf(s))'=F(s-1)\quad(s>2),\qquad (sF(s))'=f(s-1)\quad(s>3).$$
- **Starting lower continuation** ([source](#eq:functions-starting-extension)): Only at a starting lower state, and when defining its derivative weight, we continue $f$ below $2$ by $$\label{eq:functions-starting-extension} f_{\rm ext}(s)=\frac{A\log(s-1)}s\qquad(1.98\le s<2).$$
- **Dickman function** ([source](#pm-h-13)): Let $\rho$ be the Dickman function, determined by $$\rho(u)=1\quad(0\le u\le1),\qquad u\rho'(u)=-\rho(u-1)\quad(u>1).$$
- **Derivative weights** ([source](#pm-h-14)): Define $$\phi_\mathrm e(s)=s^2f'(s)\quad(s\ge2),\qquad \phi_\mathrm o(s)=-s^2F'(s)\quad(s\ge0.95).$$
- **Regenerating state** ([source](#pm-h-15)): Call an odd state with $s\le3$ a regenerating state; regeneration is viewed as occurring just before its next draw.
- **Full prime box** ([source](#pm-h-26)): A full box fixes a length and the ordered bin labels of all its positions.
- **Rational alignment and nonaligned product** ([source](#stmt-auto-definition-1)): A prime $p$ *aligns* with a reduced rational $A/D$, where $A\in\mathbb Z$, $D\in\mathbb N$, and $\gcd(A,D)=1$, if $$D a_p\equiv A\pmod p.$$ This condition implies $p\nmid D$. For a squarefree product $q$ of primes carrying prescribed classes, write $$q_E(A/D)= \prod_{\substack{\ell\mid q\\ \ell\text{ does not align with }A/D}}\ell .$$
- **Effective arithmetic parameters** ([source](#pm-h-38)): Let $b\in[1,q_f]$ be the integer CRT representative of the hit conditions at the primes of $q_f$, and define $$\label{eq:variance-effective} C_0=\frac{Db-A}{q_{f,A}}, \qquad T=\max\left(H,\frac{\|C_0\|}{R}\right).$$
- **Eligibility, ownership and tag** ([source](#stmt-def-stops-owner)): A reduced rational $A/D$, with $D>0$, is *eligible* if $$\label{eq:stops-eligible} D\le w^{C_s},\qquad \|A\|\le Yw^{C_s+2}.$$ Fix a constant $\eta>0$, to be bounded by an absolute constant below. A search bin has an *owner* if some eligible rational aligns to at least a $1-\eta$ fraction of its primes, by prime count. The owner is that rational. Along a descending-prime path, its *tag prime* is the first search-bin factor that aligns to the owner of its bin, if such a factor occurs. Its *tag rational* is this owner and is retained for the rest of the path.
- **Prefix-free lower stop** ([source](#stmt-def-stops-rule)): An included, nonempty, even node is a *stop* if it is the first node along its branch satisfying all the following conditions: 1. Its length is at most $C_{\rm len}\log B$. Its tag has been attained, the tag bin has multiplicity one in this prefix, and every prime of the prefix aligns to its tag rational. 2. Every nonempty even prefix ending at position $j$ satisfies $$\label{eq:stops-safety} r_j^{\rm rep}\ge \mathcal H(x_j^{\rm rep})+3\Delta.$$ 3. At the present node, its last representative exponent and representative ratio satisfy $$\label{eq:stops-window} b_0\le x_j^{\rm rep}\le b_1, \qquad 2.06\le r_j^{\rm rep}/x_j^{\rm rep}\le2.16.$$ The empty root is not subjected to <a href="#eq:stops-safety" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:stops-safety">[eq:stops-safety]</a>.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-uniform-jacobsthal-upper | h(k) | ≤ | $C\frac{k^2}{(\log\log(3k))^2}$ | The constant is absolute and is not made explicit. |
| q-survivor-lower | Survivor count | ≥ | $c\frac{YV_0}{B^2}$ | The lower constant and the eventual cutoff are absolute. |
| q-interval-length | Y | = | $\left\lfloor\frac{z^2}{L^2}\right\rfloor$ | Defined from the upper prime cutoff. |
| q-small-prime-cutoff | w | = | $\frac{L}{(\log L)^2}$ | Separates progression sieving from the prime-tree expansion. |
| q-logarithmic-cutoff | B | = | $\frac{L}{\log w}$ | Defined after the small-prime cutoff. |
| q-tree-length-offset | a_\star | = | $\frac1{100}$ | Fixed throughout the tree argument. |
| q-cycle-decrement-floor | G | ≥ | $\log(4/3)$ | A deterministic floor for every complete cycle. |
| q-invariant-regeneration-mass | \beta | = | $\frac{4A}{3}$ | Uses the normalisation of the sieve functions. |
| q-invariant-drift-upper | M_g | ≤ | $4e^\gamma$ | An absolute upper bound on the drift integral. |
| q-reference-full-correction | I_{\mathrm{full}} | = | $\frac83$ | The benchmark before subtracting admission losses. |
| q-reference-correction-positive | I | > | $0.14$ | After the first-omission terms and their tail are bounded. |
| q-reference-correction-sharper | I | > | $\frac{222979373}{10^9}$ | The sharper bound recorded in the remark. |
| q-reference-root-limit-margin | -a_\star+\frac{2I}{M_g} | > | $0.02$ | The explicit limit margin does not provide an explicit eventual prime cutoff. |
| q-box-prefix-movement | \Delta | = | $2C_{\rm len}\xi$ | The length constant is fixed before choosing the width. |
| q-hard-effective-modulus | Dq_{f,E} | ≤ | $w^{C_s}$ | The exponent is fixed independently of sufficiently small fixed width. |
| q-hard-normalised-intercept | \|C_0\|/R | ≤ | $w^{C_s}$ | Controls translation as well as nonaligned factors. |
| q-aligned-bin-survivor-average | \sum_{p\in\mathcal P}T_p | ≥ | $.39V_{\rm all}(P)\sum_{p\in\mathcal P}J_p$ | The window is fixed before the prime limit. |
| q-retained-error-budget | \sum_{d\in\mathcal T}\|N_d-\mu_d\| | ≤ | $\frac{3c_L}{8}\frac{YV_0}{B^2}$ | All error allocations and the fixed width precede the eventual cutoff. |
| q-elementary-bounded-range-jacobsthal | h(k) | ≤ | $(k+1)2^k+1$ | An explicit coarse bound used to absorb the remaining finite set. |

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-stops-aligned-average",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $C_s$. Take $b_0$ sufficiently large in terms of $C_s$, and let $b_1>b_0$ be fixed. For every sufficiently small fixed $\\xi>0$, the following holds for all sufficiently large $z$. Let $A/D$ be eligible in the sense of <a href=\"#eq:stops-eligible\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:stops-eligible\">[eq:stops-eligible]</a>, let $P=w^b$ be a prime with $b\\in[b_0-1,b_1]$, and let $q'$ be a squarefree product of primes at most $z$ whose least prime factor is $P$ and all of whose prime factors align to $A/D$. Let $\\mathcal P$ be a search bin, none of whose primes divides $q'$. Suppose that, for every $p\\in\\mathcal P$, $$\\label{eq:stops-length-exponents}\n J_p=\\frac{Y}{pq'},\\qquad 2.03\\le\\log_PJ_p\\le2.19.$$ Define $T_p$ to count the integers $1\\le n\\le Y$ satisfying $Dn\\equiv A\\pmod{pq'}$ and avoiding the prescribed classes at every prime $t<P$. Then $$\\label{eq:stops-all-primes-lower}\n \\sum_{p\\in\\mathcal P}T_p\n \\ge .39V_{\\rm all}(P)\\sum_{p\\in\\mathcal P}J_p.$$ There is also an absolute constant $C_U$, independent of $C_s,b_0,b_1$ and of sufficiently small fixed $\\xi$, such that $$\\label{eq:stops-individual-upper}\n T_p\\le C_UJ_pV_{\\rm all}(P)\\qquad(p\\in\\mathcal P).$$ Both estimates are uniform in the eligible rational, the bin, the product $q'$, and the prescribed residue classes.",

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-fundamental-sieve** [claim](#stmt-lem-fundamental) / [proof](#stmt-lem-fundamental): Use the stated dimension-two fundamental lemma as an external input. -> Product approximation for sifted counts.
- **step-prime-distribution** [claim](#stmt-lem-prime-inputs) / [proof](#stmt-lem-prime-inputs): Collect the classical prime-distribution inputs with their declared uniformity and possible ineffectivity. -> Prime distribution and product estimates for later transfers and averages.
- **step-small-progression-sieve** [claim](#stmt-lem-small-sieve) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-small-sieve): Apply the fundamental lemma to progression coordinates and shorten the sieve cutoff for the upper bound. -> Upper and relative small-prime counting estimates.
- **step-parity-lower-bound** [claim](#stmt-lem-tree-lower-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tree-lower-bound): Induct upward through the finite tree using parity and nonnegative omitted counts. -> A stopped lower bound with sufficient progression length at every included node.
- **step-buchstab-normalisation** [claim](#stmt-lem-elementary-buchstab) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-elementary-buchstab): Prove integrability of the derivative and identify the limit through the Laplace transform and Euler integral. -> An independently normalised Buchstab limit.
- **step-positive-future-integrals** [claim](#stmt-lem-future-integrals) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-future-integrals): Combine Dickman decay with an absorbing auxiliary chain and the independent Buchstab normalisation. -> Positive deficits and future-integral identities.
- **step-derivative-weight-kernel** [claim](#stmt-lem-derivative-weights) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-derivative-weights): Differentiate the delay equations and integrate from infinity to normalise the tilted kernel. -> Positive derivative weights with normalisation and tail control.
- **step-regeneration** [claim](#stmt-lem-regeneration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-regeneration): Use exponential drift, compact return attempts and a continuous component of a return decrement. -> Independent cycles with finite exponential moments and nonarithmetic decrements.
- **step-invariant-occupation** [claim](#stmt-lem-invariant) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-invariant): Verify incoming density identities and recover cycle occupation by stationary last-regeneration decomposition. -> Raw invariant densities, cycle occupation identity and drift bound.
- **step-continuous-renewal-limit** [claim](#stmt-prop-occupation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-occupation): Show direct Riemann integrability of the cycle response, apply key renewal and restore the uniformly tight initial delay. -> Uniform continuous compact occupation limit.
- **step-uniform-bands** [claim](#stmt-lem-band-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-band-bounds): Bound renewal mass in fixed bands and compare initial-state densities to regeneration densities. -> Uniform band estimates, including inverse derivative-weight rewards.
- **step-marked-visits** [claim](#stmt-lem-marked-visits) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-marked-visits): Mark regeneration cycles and bound wide intervals without a marked renewal using finite mean spacings. -> High-probability even visits before compact endpoints.
- **step-prime-harmonic-transfer** [claim](#stmt-lem-prime-harmonic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-prime-harmonic): Apply partial summation to the prime number theorem. -> Uniform reciprocal-prime quadrature and total prefix-mass bound.
- **step-remove-high-states** [claim](#stmt-lem-high-states) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-high-states): Use an ordered factorial tail for the many factors required after a high-ratio ancestor. -> Negligible high-state histories.
- **step-couple-prime-paths** [claim](#stmt-prop-prime-coupling) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-prime-coupling): Compare discrete and continuous bin masses, add a cemetery state and couple bin labels through the finite low-state horizon. -> A killed prime chain with matching exits and a telescoping weight identity.
- **step-prime-marginals** [claim](#stmt-lem-discrete-marginals) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-discrete-marginals): Induct on parity using cancellation of intermediate derivative weights and mesh estimates. -> Pointwise discrete marginal majorants.
- **step-prime-occupation** [claim](#stmt-prop-prime-occupation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-prime-occupation): Transfer compact tests by coupling and control unbounded inverse-weight rewards by marginal majorants and band estimates. -> Prime compact mass, event bounds, tail bounds and occupation limit.
- **step-reference-residual** [claim](#stmt-lem-ref-residual) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ref-residual): Use partial summation, product approximations and Dickman decay to sum the quadrature residual absolutely. -> Negligible summed reference residual.
- **step-boundary-anomaly** [claim](#stmt-lem-ref-anomaly) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ref-anomaly): Transfer bounded-length boundary polynomials and compare first omissions with a factorial tail. -> Uniform compact boundary convergence and rapidly decaying anomalies.
- **step-positive-reference-margin** [claim](#stmt-prop-reference-margin) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-reference-margin): Transport the boundary anomaly by occupation, subtract the first-omission losses and combine with the displaced root benchmark. -> A positive eventual reference root margin.
- **step-local-reference** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-reference) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-reference): Bound the absolute propagated boundary anomaly and then transfer fixed-window polynomials to primes. -> Uniform local reference comparison near the lower-sieve threshold.
- **step-recent-edge-witness** [claim](#stmt-lem-edge-witness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-edge-witness): Cross the fixed long-progression threshold and telescope normalised errors along the remaining bounded edge segment. -> A recent witnessing edge for every bad compact endpoint.
- **step-regular-prime-boxes** [claim](#stmt-prop-regular-boxes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-regular-boxes): Exclude long, repeated-bin and near-threshold histories, then enlarge to full boxes using a shifted starting gap. -> Independent candidate boxes with width-independent total mass.
- **step-affine-intersection-bound** [claim](#stmt-lem-elementary-bezout) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-elementary-bezout): Choose suitable coordinates and bound the degree of the nonzero Sylvester resultant. -> A coefficient-independent bound on distinct affine intersections.
- **step-nonlinear-lattice-points** [claim](#stmt-lem-nonlinear-points) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-nonlinear-points): Separate complex components and decompose real nonlinear components into monotone strictly convex or concave arcs. -> A degree-uniform nonlinear lattice-point estimate.
- **step-separated-arcs-sieve** [claim](#stmt-lem-elementary-large-sieve) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-elementary-large-sieve): Integrate over disjoint arcs and apply Cauchy–Schwarz and Parseval. -> The additive large-sieve order needed by the inverse estimate.
- **step-fixed-rational-inverse-list** [claim](#stmt-prop-inverse) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-nonlinear-points-2): Fix the whole-box list, freeze residue tests, sample incidences, interpolate a small-height polynomial and extract a rational line using pair collisions. -> Rational alignment and nonaligned-product control outside a whole-box exception.
- **step-random-stable-variance** [claim](#stmt-lem-random-variance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-random-variance): Expand the pair survival factor into a convergent divisor series and then use the moment identity for finite dominating measures. -> Independent and stable relative variance estimates.
- **step-progression-upper-sieve** [claim](#stmt-lem-elementary-brun-titchmarsh) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-elementary-brun-titchmarsh): Sieve progression coordinates at a fixed power of their length and absorb the endpoint remainders. -> A prime progression upper bound uniform in the modulus.
- **step-hyperbola-completion** [claim](#stmt-lem-hyperbola) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hyperbola): Complete interval weights by Fourier inversion and bound the frequency cases while retaining the unit restriction and gcd factors. -> A uniform modular hyperbola count with translated intervals and multiplicities.
- **step-effective-size-alternative** [claim](#stmt-prop-structured-variance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-structured-variance): Dominate small-modulus slices by progression bounds and large-modulus slices by sieved hyperbola counts, then apply stable variance. -> Few bad aligning isolated primes when effective size is large.
- **step-classify-hard-endpoints** [claim](#stmt-cor-hard-endpoints) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-hard-endpoints): Union over bounded candidate lists and candidates using full-box mass, then convert effective-size bounds to global rational height. -> Hard endpoint classification outside a prescribed scaled mass.
- **step-euler-probability** [claim](#stmt-lem-stops-euler-probability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-stops-euler-probability): Represent Euler weights as independent prime valuations and compare them monotonically with ordinary geometric valuations. -> A uniform truncated Euler-weight lower bound.
- **step-aligned-bin-average** [claim](#stmt-lem-stops-aligned-average) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-stops-euler-probability-2): Factor the auxiliary integer into a small permitted part and a large prime, average the varying search prime and retain uniform remainders. -> A whole-bin survivor lower average and an individual upper bound.
- **step-compare-actual-stops** [claim](#stmt-prop-stopped-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-stopped-comparison): Partition by tag position and other factors, preserve the whole owner-aligning subset and subtract the small nonaligning contribution. -> Nonnegative aggregate stopped-subtree correction.
- **step-cover-hard-endpoints** [claim](#stmt-prop-hard-stopping) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-hard-stopping): Identify the correct tag in independent search bins and use marked renewal visits with representative slack. -> An arbitrarily small scaled mass of hard endpoints without stopped prefixes.
- **step-assemble-survivors** [claim](#stmt-thm-survivors) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-hard-stopping-2): Choose the error budget in the prescribed order, fix the width last and combine the stopped identity with the positive reference margin. -> The uniform quantitative survivor lower bound.
- **step-deduce-jacobsthal** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-hard-stopping-3): Choose the prime cutoff by the divisor count, bound deletion by remaining large divisor primes and absorb the bounded range by inclusion–exclusion. -> The uniform Jacobsthal upper bound.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-uniform-coprime-window**: The bound for the coprime search window is uniform over the prime divisors and the translation of the interval.
- **invariant-arbitrary-forbidden-classes**: The quantitative survivor lower bound holds for every assignment of a forbidden residue class at each prime below the cutoff.
- **invariant-parity-and-prefix-free-stops**: The admitted alternating expansion remains a lower bound when exact survivor counts replace subtrees at a prefix-free collection of included lower nodes.
- **invariant-aggregate-stopped-comparison**: After the prescribed parameter choices, the sum of exact survivor counts at the stops dominates the sum of their reference subtree values.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-explicit-search-budget**: Turn the improved asymptotic bound directly into a numerical retry limit for a concrete coprime search. -> The absolute constant and the eventual thresholds are not supplied as explicit numerical inputs.
- **anti-use-reference-positivity-alone**: Infer actual sieve survivors solely from positivity of the reference tree. -> The discrepancy between the reference counts and the prescribed residue classes remains uncontrolled.
- **anti-use-shrinking-bin-width**: Let the bin width shrink with the prime cutoff while retaining the stated uniform estimates. -> The eventual thresholds may depend on the fixed width, so the stated order of limits does not justify a varying width.
- **anti-use-independent-actual-classes**: Treat the actual forbidden classes as independent random choices when applying the variance argument. -> The auxiliary independent models dominate suitable measures; they do not describe the distribution of the actual prescribed classes.
- **anti-use-pointwise-stop-domination**: Assume every stopped node individually has an exact survivor count at least its reference value. -> The established comparison is an aggregate inequality obtained by averaging over the owner-aligning subset of a prime bin.
- **anti-use-primorial-only-validation**: Validate the uniform coprime-window guarantee using only primorial moduli. -> A theorem about the first primes does not establish the extremal bound for arbitrary prime sets.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The principal results are uniform existence bounds over unbounded moduli and asymptotic survivor estimates, rather than a finite constructive computation with explicit thresholds. The source states: "The constants in the proof are absolute after the explicitly stated parameters have been chosen. They need not be effective." The finite decreasing-prime expansion supplies a proof component, but checking finite instances of that expansion would not certify the uniform theorem or its asymptotic comparison.

| other (computational number theory) | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The elementary inclusion–exclusion argument supplies a finite coprime-search window in terms of the number of distinct prime divisors. Enumerating that interval and checking coprimality gives a concrete finite search, although it does not implement the improved asymptotic bound with an explicit constant. |

Put $$\\label{eq:boxes-delta}\n \\Delta=2C_{\\rm len}\\xi.$$ For each fixed $0<\\xi\\le\\xi_0$ and sufficiently large $z$, there is a family $\\mathcal G$ of regular included endpoints with $r_d\\le K$ such that $$\\label{eq:boxes-exception}\n \\mathfrak m\\bigl(\\{d\\text{ included}:r_d\\le K\\}\\setminus\\mathcal G\\bigr)\n \\le \\frac{\\vartheta+C_{\\rm th}\\Delta+o_\\xi(1)}{B^2}.$$ The term $o_\\xi(1)$ tends to zero after all fixed parameters, including $\\xi$, have been chosen. The statement is uniform in the prescribed residue classes. The regular endpoints and their covering full boxes have these additional properties.*\n\n1.  *Every regular endpoint has length at most $C_{\\rm len}\\log B$ and has an isolated position. Every used search bin, including its isolated bin, has multiplicity one. Every position satisfying $$x_j\\le2K_*+3,\\qquad r_{j-1}\\le2K_*+5$$ also has multiplicity one. The isolated position precedes every edge supplied by Lemma <a href=\"#lem:edge-witness\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:edge-witness\">25</a>.*\n\n\n\n2.  *For every nonempty even prefix of a regular endpoint, $$\\label{eq:boxes-threshold-clearance}\n     r_j-\\mathcal H(x_j)>10\\Delta.$$ Moving prime choices anywhere within their fixed bins changes any prefix gap by at most $\\Delta$, and changes a threshold expression $r_j-\\mathcal H(x_j)$ by at most $\\Delta+2h$. In particular the representative gaps and exponents of every regular box satisfy $$\\label{eq:boxes-representative-safety}\n     r_j^{\\rm rep}\\ge \\mathcal H(x_j^{\\rm rep})+3\\Delta\n     \\quad\\text{at every nonempty even prefix}.$$*\n\n\n3.  *Let $\\mathfrak B$ be the distinct full boxes containing at least one regular endpoint. There is a constant $C_K^{\\rm box}$, depending only on a fixed enlargement of the endpoint compact range and the allowed starting ratios, such that $$\\label{eq:boxes-expanded-mass}\n     \\sum_{\\mathcal B\\in\\mathfrak B}\\mathfrak m(\\mathcal B)\n           \\le\\frac{C_K^{\\rm box}}{B^2}.$$ In particular this constant is independent of $\\xi$, $\\vartheta$, and the total number of boxes. Every tuple in these boxes is a standard path from initial gap $r_0+1$, ends at gap at most $K+2$ for that starting gap, and has its actual final $\\log_wJ\\ge3a_\\star/4$.*\n\n\n\n\n4.  *In a regular box, declare an edge a candidate if it is among the last $H_e=\\lceil2K_*+10\\rceil$ positions, is after the isolated position, has multiplicity one, and can satisfy the size bounds in <a href=\"#eq:boxes-witness\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:boxes-witness\">[eq:boxes-witness]</a> for some tuple in the box. There are at most $H_e$ candidates, and every witness for a regular endpoint is among them. Throughout the whole box of each candidate, writing its parent as $d'=pq$ and its prime as $u$, one has $$\\label{eq:boxes-candidate-slack}\n     \\log_w\\frac{Y}{pq}\\le2K_*+4,\n     \\qquad\n     \\log_w\\frac{Y}{pqu}\\ge\\frac{3a_\\star}{4}.$$ The bins for $p$ and $u$ have lower endpoints $R$ and $U$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:boxes-prime-ranges}\n     z^{\\alpha_{\\min}}\\le R\\le z^{\\alpha_{\\max}},\n     \\qquad w\\le U\\le w^{2K_*+4}.$$ The choices of $p$, $q$, $u$, and the descendants after this edge are independent under the full-box measure. All their prime factors are disjoint. If $S_q$ is the product of the bin upper endpoints for the factors of $q$, with their specified multiplicities, then $$\\label{eq:boxes-sq}\n     1\\le\\frac{S_q}{q}\\le(1+\\xi)^{C_{\\rm len}\\log B}.$$*\n\n5.  *Every regular box has at least $c_7\\log w$ search positions, for an absolute $c_7>0$ and sufficiently large $z$. These positions have distinct bins. All its nonsearch positions follow all its search positions.",

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-unspecified-improved-constant** (nonconstructive): The improved uniform bound does not supply an explicit numerical constant or an executable numerical search horizon. Blocked use: ['Assigning a concrete retry budget directly from the improved asymptotic estimate.', 'Treating the improved bound as an explicit table of finite Jacobsthal values.']
- **limit-survivor-cutoff-and-interval-scale** (hypothesis): The survivor estimate requires a sufficiently large prime cutoff and the interval length and auxiliary scales prescribed in the paper. Blocked use: ['Applying the survivor lower bound at an arbitrary finite cutoff without establishing its threshold.', 'Substituting an unrelated interval length into the stated survivor estimate.']
- **limit-single-forbidden-class** (hypothesis): The main survivor theorem prescribes a single forbidden residue class at each prime. The broader local-density conditions of the fundamental lemma do not automatically extend the main survivor theorem to multiple exclusions. Blocked use: ['Applying the main survivor estimate to unrestricted multiple forbidden classes at each prime.', 'Replacing the coprimality covering problem by an arbitrary polynomial filter without proving a new reduction.']
- **limit-coprime-progression-step-and-length** (hypothesis): The progression sieve requires its step to be coprime to the sieve primes and its coordinate length to satisfy the stated growth conditions. Its relative approximation requires a stronger logarithmic-length condition than its upper bound. Blocked use: ['Using the progression estimate when the step has a sieve prime divisor.', 'Assuming the relative approximation follows merely from the shorter-length upper-bound regime.']
- **limit-parity-admission-and-stopping** (hypothesis): The alternating expansion is a lower bound under the stated admission rule. Exact subtree replacement is justified at a prefix-free collection of included lower nodes. Blocked use: ['Stopping at upper nodes while retaining the lower-bound interpretation.', 'Expanding descendants of a node whose exact survivor count has already replaced its subtree.', 'Dropping the admission test without re-establishing the progression-length and sign arguments.']
- **limit-reference-positivity-needs-comparison** (formal_scope): A positive reference-tree value alone does not establish a positive survivor count for the actual prescribed residue classes. The retained counting errors and stopped differences must also be controlled. Blocked use: ['Using the reference margin as a direct lower bound for the original sieve.', 'Omitting the discrepancy terms from the stopped comparison.']
- **limit-starting-extension-only** (formal_scope): The continued lower-sieve benchmark below its ordinary threshold is used only at a starting lower state and in its derivative weight. It is not the benchmark at an omitted lower child. Blocked use: ["Evaluating an omitted lower child's benchmark with the negative starting continuation.", 'Extending the continuation to arbitrary states beyond its declared range.']
- **limit-renewal-tests-and-starts** (hypothesis): The compact occupation limit applies to admissible tests and the declared starting ratios. Its extension to boundary indicators uses the specified admissibility conditions. Blocked use: ['Applying the compact occupation limit to arbitrary unbounded rewards.', 'Claiming the same uniformity over unrestricted starting ratios.', 'Treating an arbitrary discontinuous test as admissible.']
- **limit-coupling-does-not-control-inverse-weight-tails** (approximation): Total variation coupling alone does not control rewards containing inverse derivative weights far into the ratio tail. The proof additionally uses pointwise marginal majorants and occupation-band estimates. Blocked use: ['Transferring an unbounded inverse-weight reward solely from the coupling failure probability.', 'Discarding high-ratio histories without the corresponding weighted estimates.']
- **limit-fixed-width-before-prime-limit** (hypothesis): Bin widths and cutoff windows are fixed before taking the prime limit. Constants independent of sufficiently small fixed widths do not establish uniformity for shrinking widths. Blocked use: ['Letting the bin width tend to zero with the prime cutoff under the stated fixed-width estimates.', 'Choosing the local cutoff window after observing the prime-limit parameter.']
- **limit-independent-full-box-measure** (hypothesis): The inverse and variance arguments use independence under the full-box measure with the required singleton bins and disjoint prime factors. Conditioning on a discrepant endpoint does not preserve that independence automatically. Blocked use: ['Varying a candidate prime independently when its bin has repeated positions.', 'Assuming independence after conditioning the full-box measure on hardness or discrepancy.']
- **limit-whole-box-exception-normalisation** (formal_scope): The inverse exceptional fraction is measured against the whole candidate box, rather than against the discrepant subset. Summing these exceptions uses the total full-box mass and bounded candidate lists. Blocked use: ['Interpreting the inverse exceptional fraction as a conditional failure rate among bad edges.', 'Multiplying each exceptional fraction by the number of boxes instead of summing their harmonic measures.']
- **limit-actual-classes-are-not-independent** (hypothesis): The actual prescribed classes remain arbitrary. The independent forbidden-class model transfers through the stated domination conditions, including exact vanishing when the comparison pair probability is zero. Blocked use: ['Assuming the actual prescribed classes are independent random variables.', 'Applying the stable variance estimate without its total-mass, single-point and pair bounds.', 'Replacing a required exact zero pair probability by a merely small positive error.']
- **limit-hard-classification-concerns-included-endpoints** (formal_scope): The hard-endpoint classification concerns actual included regular compact endpoints outside its exceptional harmonic mass. Enlarged full boxes dominate measure and do not make every contained tuple a hard endpoint. Blocked use: ['Applying the hard-endpoint conclusion to every tuple in an enlarged full box.', 'Dropping regularity or inclusion when using the global rational-height conclusions.']
- **limit-stopped-domination-is-aggregate** (formal_scope): The stopped comparison is a summed inequality obtained by averaging over the owner-aligning subset. It does not assert domination at each individual stop. Blocked use: ['Replacing the aggregate comparison by a pointwise inequality at every stopped node.', 'Restricting an averaging group to an arbitrary subset of its owner-aligning primes.']
- **limit-hyperbola-counts-integers-not-primes** (formal_scope): The modular hyperbola count counts integer pairs under its coprimality and residue restrictions. A separate sieve is required to upper-bound prime coordinates, and interval multiplicities remain part of the count. Blocked use: ['Reading the hyperbola main term as a prime-pair asymptotic.', 'Reducing long intervals to residue sets while discarding multiplicities.', 'Removing the unit restrictions or the coprimality condition on the divisor parameter.']
- **limit-nonlinear-components-and-coprime-curves** (hypothesis): The nonlinear lattice-point bound excludes points lying only on line components. The affine intersection bound requires no common nonconstant factor. Blocked use: ['Applying the nonlinear point estimate to an unrestricted line component.', 'Using the affine intersection bound for curves sharing a component.']
- **limit-large-divisor-primes-need-deletion-budget** (formal_scope): The survivor theorem at a prime cutoff is only an intermediate step in the uniform Jacobsthal bound. The deduction must control survivors removed by the remaining divisor primes. Blocked use: ['Treating avoidance of primes below the cutoff as coprimality to a modulus with larger prime divisors.', 'Deducing the uniform modulus result from survivor positivity alone without its quantitative surplus.']
- **limit-primorial-extremality-is-distinct** (counterexample_scope): Primorial extremality is distinct from the uniform quadratic-bound question. A result restricted to primorials does not establish the maximum over arbitrary prime sets. Blocked use: ['Validating the uniform theorem solely on primorial inputs.', 'Assuming primorials always realise the largest coprime gap.']
- **limit-historical-lower-bound-is-existential** (counterexample_scope): The historical large-gap lower bound is an existence construction, rather than a lower bound for every modulus with the prescribed number of distinct prime divisors. Blocked use: ["Assigning the extremal construction's lower bound to every individual modulus.", 'Using the historical lower construction as a uniform minimum search cost.']
- **limit-finite-checks-do-not-certify-uniform-asymptotics** (formal_scope): Finite checks of the tree expansion or the elementary coprime-search bound do not certify the improved uniform theorem, its asymptotic comparisons or its unspecified thresholds. Blocked use: ['Reporting finite-instance agreement as a certificate of the improved uniform asymptotic bound.', 'Conflating the explicit elementary bound with the improved iterated-logarithmic estimate.']

For each fixed $\\eta>0$, uniformly when $J\\ge u^\\eta$, its sifted count $N$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:small-upper}\n N\\ll_\\eta JV_{\\mathrm{sm}}(u).$$ There are absolute $c_1,C_1>0$ and a sufficiently large fixed $v_0$ such that, writing $v=\\log_uJ$, uniformly for $v\\ge v_0$, $$\\label{eq:small-relative}\n \\left|\\frac{N}{JV_{\\mathrm{sm}}(u)}-1\\right|\\le C_1e^{-c_1v}.$$ All estimates are independent of the progression’s initial point, step and forbidden classes.",

        }

          "exact_quote": "Fix $K$ and $K_*>K+10$. In particular the representative gaps and exponents of every regular box satisfy $$\\label{eq:boxes-representative-safety}\n     r_j^{\\rm rep}\\ge \\mathcal H(x_j^{\\rm rep})+3\\Delta\n     \\quad\\text{at every nonempty even prefix}.$$*\n\n\n3.  *Let $\\mathfrak B$ be the distinct full boxes containing at least one regular endpoint. There is a constant $C_K^{\\rm box}$, depending only on a fixed enlargement of the endpoint compact range and the allowed starting ratios, such that $$\\label{eq:boxes-expanded-mass}\n     \\sum_{\\mathcal B\\in\\mathfrak B}\\mathfrak m(\\mathcal B)\n           \\le\\frac{C_K^{\\rm box}}{B^2}.$$ In particular this constant is independent of $\\xi$, $\\vartheta$, and the total number of boxes. Every tuple in these boxes is a standard path from initial gap $r_0+1$, ends at gap at most $K+2$ for that starting gap, and has its actual final $\\log_wJ\\ge3a_\\star/4$.*\n\n\n\n\n4.  *In a regular box, declare an edge a candidate if it is among the last $H_e=\\lceil2K_*+10\\rceil$ positions, is after the isolated position, has multiplicity one, and can satisfy the size bounds in <a href=\"#eq:boxes-witness\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:boxes-witness\">[eq:boxes-witness]</a> for some tuple in the box. All their prime factors are disjoint. If $S_q$ is the product of the bin upper endpoints for the factors of $q$, with their specified multiplicities, then $$\\label{eq:boxes-sq}\n     1\\le\\frac{S_q}{q}\\le(1+\\xi)^{C_{\\rm len}\\log B}.$$*\n\n5.  *Every regular box has at least $c_7\\log w$ search positions, for an absolute $c_7>0$ and sufficiently large $z$. Let $\\mu$ be a finite positive measure carrying indicators $I_j$, $j\\in\\mathcal J$, and let $S=\\sum_j I_j$. Suppose, with an absolute fixed implied constant, $$\\mu(\\Omega)=1+O(\\zeta),\\qquad\n \\int I_j\\,d\\mu=V_0(1+O(\\zeta)),$$ and, for distinct $i,j$, the joint probability has the upper bound $$\\int I_iI_j\\,d\\mu\n \\le(1+O(\\zeta))\\,\\mathbb P_{\\rm ind}(I_i=I_j=1).$$ Here $\\mathbb P_{\\rm ind}$ denotes the independent forbidden-class model in the first part of the lemma.       "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-hyperbola",
          "exact_quote": "Let $H,T_0,T_1$ be positive integers such that $H\\ge2$, $T_0$ is squarefree, every prime divisor of $T_0$ divides $H$, and $(T_1,HT_0)=1$. Put $N=HT_0$. Let $C_0\\in\\mathbb Z$ satisfy $(C_0,H)=1$, and prescribe the residue pattern $$\\label{eq:hyperbola-pattern}\n p_0\\in(\\mathbb Z/T_0\\mathbb Z)^\\times,\\qquad k_0\\in\\mathbb Z/T_0\\mathbb Z,\\qquad\n p_1\\in(\\mathbb Z/T_1\\mathbb Z)^\\times,\\qquad m_1\\in\\mathbb Z/T_1\\mathbb Z.$$ The residue classes modulo $1$ have their usual unique interpretation. Let $I,J$ be bounded real intervals of lengths $X,Y_2\\ge0$, with either endpoint independently included or excluded. For a positive integer $l$ with $(l,HT_1)=1$, let $C_l$ be the number of integer pairs $(p,m)$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:hyperbola-counted-set}\n\\begin{gathered}\n p\\in I,\\qquad m\\in J,\\qquad l\\mid p,\\\\\n p\\equiv p_0\\pmod{T_0},\\qquad\n p\\equiv p_1\\pmod{T_1},\\qquad\n m\\equiv m_1\\pmod{T_1},\\\\\n pm\\equiv C_0+Hk_0\\pmod N.\n\\end{gathered}$$ Here $p$ is an integer variable, without a primality condition. Uniformly in all these data, $$\\label{eq:hyperbola-explicit-count}\n C_l=\n \\frac{XY_2\\varphi(N)}{lT_1^2N^2\\varphi(T_0)}\n +O\\left(\n \\tau(N)^2\\log^2(2N)\\sqrt{NT_0}\n \\left(1+\\frac{X}{lN}+\\frac{Y_2}{N}\\right)\n \\right),$$ with an absolute implied constant.*\n\n\n\n*In particular, suppose that $R\\to\\infty$ and the data range uniformly over families satisfying $$\\label{eq:hyperbola-application-range}\n R^{4/5}<H\\le R\\mathcal Z^{11},\\qquad\n X\\ll R,\\qquad Y_2\\le3H,\\qquad\n \\mathcal Z,T_0,T_1=R^{o(1)}.$$ Then, uniformly over all the patterns and intervals above and all squarefree $l\\le R^{1/10}$ with $(l,HT_1)=1$, $$\\label{eq:hyperbola-application-count}\n C_l=\n \\frac{XY_2\\varphi(N)}{lT_1^2N^2\\varphi(T_0)}\n +O\\bigl(R^{3/5+o(1)}\\bigr).$$ Thus the same assertion holds with error $O(R^{7/10+o(1)})$. Uniformity in the last two assertions means that the upper bounds represented by $R^{o(1)}$ in <a href=\"#eq:hyperbola-application-range\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:hyperbola-application-range\">[eq:hyperbola-application-range]</a> hold uniformly over the family; equivalently, for every $\\delta>0$ the error in <a href=\"#eq:hyperbola-application-count\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:hyperbola-application-count\">[eq:hyperbola-application-count]</a> is $O_\\delta(R^{3/5+\\delta})$ for sufficiently large $R$ in that family.           "exact_quote": "Fix a prime divisor $q>z$ of $n$. Its divisibility class has at most $1+Y/q\\le1+X$ representatives in the coordinate interval. Enclose them in a progression segment whose coordinate length is $\\lceil X\\rceil+2$. Fix a sufficiently small absolute $\\theta>0$ and sieve this segment by primes at most $X^\\theta$. This cutoff is below $z$ for large $k$, the step $q$ is invertible modulo every sieve prime, and the integers already counted in <a href=\"#eq:assembly-survivor-lower\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:assembly-survivor-lower\">[eq:assembly-survivor-lower]</a> avoid its prescribed class there. Lemma <a href=\"#lem:small-sieve\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:small-sieve\">5</a> and Mertens’ formula therefore give $$\\label{eq:large-prime-deletion}\n \\#\\{\\text{these survivors divisible by }q\\}\n \\le C_1\\left(1+\\frac X{\\log X}\\right)$$ for an absolute $C_1$, uniform in $q$, $A_0$, the classes, and the translation once the displayed length conditions hold. More explicitly, the enclosing coordinate length is comparable to $X$ and at least $(X^\\theta)^{1/(2\\theta)}$ for large $X$, so the fixed exponent upper bound in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:small-sieve\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:small-sieve\">5</a> applies. Enclosing a shorter or empty progression only increases the count; in particular the argument also covers $q>Y$.",
          "source_id": "PM-021.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "limit-large-divisor-primes-need-deletion-budget",
      "kind": "formal_scope",
      "statement": "The survivor theorem at a prime cutoff is only an intermediate step in the uniform Jacobsthal bound. The deduction must control survivors removed by the remaining divisor primes.",
      "support": "inferred"
    },
    {
      "blocked_use": [
        "Validating the uniform theorem solely on primorial inputs.",
        "Assuming primorials always realise the largest coprime gap."
      ],
      "evidence": [

          "exact_quote": "Thus the primorial extremality conjecture is distinct from the quadratic-bound question, and a theorem only about $j(P_k)$ does not settle the uniform problem.",

        }
      ],
      "id": "limit-primorial-extremality-is-distinct",
      "kind": "counterexample_scope",

      "support": "stated"
    },
    {

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 021 (`lean/docs/021.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [A quadratic bound for Jacobsthal's function](../../preprints/A-quadratic-bound-for-Jacobsthals-function-September-25-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> Let $h(k)$ be the least interval length that guarantees an integer coprime to any prescribed positive modulus with at most $k$ distinct prime factors. The formalization proves the paper's strengthened Jacobsthal bound $h(k)\le Ck^2/(\log\log(3k))^2$ for one absolute $C>0$ and every $k\ge1$. Intervals may begin at any signed integer. The earlier quadratic bound is also retained as a separate statement; the optimal order of growth is not determined.

| declaration | kind |
|---|---|
| `IsJacobsthalBound` | def |
| `JacobsthalQuadratic` | def |
| `erdos_970_quadratic` | theorem |

Comparator source `Jacobsthal.lean` (630 bytes, sha256 `a2fe296ccf4e70242e6ce3db976d236525f7ca823e4a9a5c6fede1335ca0031e`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace Erdos970

namespace NumberTheoryLean.Targets

open Filter
open scoped Topology

def IsJacobsthalBound (k m : ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∀ n : ℕ, 0 < n → n.primeFactors.card ≤ k →
    ∀ a : ℤ, ∃ i : ℕ, i < m ∧ (a + i).natAbs.Coprime n

def JacobsthalQuadratic : Prop :=
  ∃ C : ℝ, 0 < C ∧ ∀ k : ℕ, 0 < k →
    ∃ m : ℕ, IsJacobsthalBound k m ∧ (m : ℝ) ≤ C * (k : ℝ) ^ 2

end NumberTheoryLean.Targets

namespace Erdos970Final

theorem erdos_970_quadratic : NumberTheoryLean.Targets.JacobsthalQuadratic := by
  sorry

end Erdos970Final

end Erdos970

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Let $`h(k)`$ be the least integer such that every interval of $`h(k)`$ consecutive integers contains an integer coprime to any prescribed positive integer having at most <i>k</i> distinct prime divisors. We prove $`h(k)\ll k^2/(\log\log(3k))^2`$, giving an affirmative answer to Jacobsthal's quadratic-bound question.
